# Codex verification brief — sentence-level claim check (commit 0c441e0)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. You may write scratch
files only under `audit_evidence_claims/`. End with a verdict line, exactly one of:

    VERDICT: APPROVE
    VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED

## What changed

`OncoKG.check_groundedness` (src/query.py) checks a *generated* answer against the
evidence retrieved for the question. Before this commit it only checked that names and
numbers appeared somewhere in the evidence, so "AstraZeneca has discontinued
osimertinib", "osimertinib does not act on EGFR" and "osimertinib is safe" passed.

Now, given `linker=self.link_entities`, it also calls `claims.check_claims`
(src/claims.py), which reads the answer sentence by sentence:

1. A stated relation (acts on / evaluates / sponsors) must hold between the same
   entities in an evidence statement of that relation.
2. Negations, comparisons, hedges and "develops/owns/markets" are reported unless the
   sentence is quoting an evidence statement about the same entities.
3. "phase N" and "N trials" must be stated for that sentence's own entities (a named
   trial is the most specific subject).
4. Every other content word must be grammar (FUNCTION list) or appear in an evidence
   statement about the sentence's entities.

Also: `link_entities` no longer joins several short words into a one-word drug
abbreviation ("is a" was read as "isa" = isatuximab).

The goal set by the owner: ordinary-word claims ("safe", "discontinued") and relations
must be caught. The design is deliberately strict: a true fact the graph does not hold
is reported as unverifiable, and a human reviewer decides.

## What to verify (reproduce everything; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **False negatives (most important).** Find answers containing a claim the retrieved
   evidence does not support that still return `grounded=True`. Use the real graph:
   `python src/app.py` exposes `Engine().check(question, answer)`; or call
   `OncoKG().check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, True, vocab, linker=kg.link_entities)`.
   Try at least: reversed relations, wrong drug–target / company–drug / trial–drug
   pairing in multi-entity sentences, pronouns ("it", "this drug"), relations stated
   with nouns ("an EGFR inhibitor", "AstraZeneca's osimertinib"), status/phase of the
   wrong trial, counts, negation spelled differently ("isn't", "no longer",
   "un-", "non-"), clinical claims made only of words that happen to be in evidence
   statements, claims split across sentences, lists, Korean or mixed text, markdown.
B. **False positives.** Faithful answers an LLM would plausibly write from the evidence
   (paraphrases, "is an inhibitor of", "is being tested in", lists) that are flagged.
   Strict is intended, but a check that flags most faithful answers is not useful —
   say how often it happens on realistic paraphrases of each demo question in
   `src/query.py:main`.
C. **Correctness of the code**: regexes, sentence splitting, the subject-inheritance
   rule for fragments of one line, `quoted()` bigram rule (can it be abused to launder a
   negation?), dedup in `check_groundedness` (one finding per value — can it hide a
   finding?), the `link_entities` change (does it break any multi-word drug name the
   pipeline should still link? check against the dictionary).
D. **Regressions**: `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (79 expected), and
   that the template answers for the demo questions, read as prose, still pass.
E. **Documentation honesty**: README.md / README.ko.md item 13, the docstrings in
   claims.py and query.py, web/template.html section 06, web/try.html box 3 — do they
   claim more than the code does?

## Report format

`BLOCKERS` (claims that pass and should not, or wrong code), `SHOULD FIX`, `NOTES`,
each with a reproducible input and the observed output. Then the verdict line.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

1. **Relations are checked without sentence direction or required endpoints.**

2. **Pronouns can lose their subject when the next sentence names an object.**  
   `src/claims.py:129` replaces inherited entities whenever *any* entity is found. Missing relation endpoints then pass unchecked.

   - Q3: `osimertinib acts on EGFR. It inhibits ALK.`
   - Q3: `osimertinib acts on EGFR. This drug inhibits ALK.`

   The ALK claim passes both on one line and with a newline before `It`. The trial is **PHASE2**; mentioning osimertinib in the second sentence replaces the trial antecedent and admits other osimertinib trials??phases.

   Controls: `osimertinib inhibits ALK.` fails, and `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib. It is phase 3.` fails.

3. **Evidence scope pools different entities, allowing phases, statuses and counts to migrate.**  
   `src/claims.py:138` selects statements mentioning **any** sentence entity; `src/claims.py:208` and `src/claims.py:218` accept a matching value anywhere in that pool.

   | Question | Exact answer | Retrieved evidence |
   |---|---|---|
   | Q2 | `NCT03909334 is phase 3 and NCT07680790 is phase 2.` | Phases are respectively **2** and **3**. |
   | Q2 | `NCT03909334 and NCT07680790 are recruiting.` | First is **ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING**; second is RECRUITING. |
   | Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors 10 NSCLC trials evaluating osimertinib.` | Osimertinib count is **14**; **10** belongs to carboplatin. |

   | Q4 | `PLB1003 is approved for all lung cancer and acts on ALK.` | PLB1003 has **PHASE_1**; APPROVAL comes from other ALK drugs. |

4. **`quoted()` launders development and negation claims through matching bigrams.**  
   `src/claims.py:148`, `src/claims.py:155`, `src/claims.py:201`.

   Q1 answers:

   - `AstraZeneca is developing the drug osimertinib.`
   - `AstraZeneca has evidence of developing osimertinib.`
   - `This is not evidence that AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib.`

   The evidence says **?쏿 sponsorship, not evidence of developing the drug.??* Matching `developing the`, `of developing`, or `not evidence` exempts the changed claim without checking its meaning or negation scope.

5. **Hyphenated `non-` negation disappears.**  
   `non` is in `FUNCTION` at `src/claims.py:70`; tokenization at `src/claims.py:102` separates it from the following word.

   - Q3: `Osimertinib is a non-inhibitor of EGFR.`
   - Q2: `NCT07680790 is non-recruiting.`

   Evidence states EGFR inhibition and **RECRUITING**, respectively.

6. **Markdown brackets and non-English text can bypass claim checking entirely.**  
   `src/claims.py:87` removes arbitrary bracket contents; `src/claims.py:102` and `src/claims.py:111` only recognize ASCII words.

   All with Q3:

   - `Osimertinib [is safe and discontinued] acts on EGFR.`
   - `[osimertinib is safe](osimertinib) acts on EGFR.`
   - `?ㅼ떆癒명떚?숈? ?덉쟾?섎ŉ 遺?묒슜???녿떎.`

   These are skipped claims, not merely difficult semantic paraphrases.

7. **Allowed words and relation synonyms still permit unsupported clinical claims.**

   - Q3: `Osimertinib is a treatment for all cancer.` passes because the clinical assertion consists of `FUNCTION` words (`src/claims.py:65`, `src/claims.py:225`). The evidence only states EGFR inhibition.
   - Question: `What does omaveloxolone target?`  
     Answer: `Omaveloxolone inhibits NFE2L2.`  
     Evidence: `OMAVELOXOLONE acts on NFE2L2 (activator)`.

   The second example exposes loss of action type: inhibition and other relation words map to generic `ACTS_ON` at `src/claims.py:35`; `src/claims.py:185` does not verify the stated action.

8. **Numeric regexes accept partial phases and miss ordinary count layouts.**

   | Question | Exact answer | Failure |
   |---|---|---|
   | Q4 | `PLB1003 is phase 13.` | Phase regex reads only **phase 1**; global number check finds 13 elsewhere. |
   | Q2 | `NCT07680790 is phase 3b.` | Regex validates **phase 3**; one-letter suffix is ignored. |
   | Q4 | `PLB1003 has 13 non small cell lung cancer trials.` | Count regex allows at most two intervening words. |
   | Q4 | `"- PLB1003: NSCLC trials: 13\n- lorlatinib: NSCLC trials: 0"` | Number-after-label layout bypasses count checking. |

"""Clause-level claim check for a generated answer.

The name-and-number check in query.py catches invented drugs and invented
figures. It cannot catch a false statement built from true tokens:
"AstraZeneca has discontinued osimertinib", "osimertinib does not act on EGFR",
"osimertinib is safe for all patients", "EGFR acts on osimertinib". This module
reads each clause the way a reviewer would: who is it about, what does it say
about them, and which retrieved statement says the same about the same entities?

A clause passes only when all of the following hold.

  * Its relations (acts on / inhibits / activates, evaluates, sponsors) run in the
    stated direction between the stated entities, and an evidence statement of that
    relation names both. Inhibiting or activating must match the stated action.
  * Its phase, status, counts and every other content word appear in an evidence
    statement that names every entity of the clause, so a value cannot be borrowed
    from another trial or drug mentioned in the same sentence or the same evidence.
  * It contains no negation, comparison, hedge or development/ownership claim. The
    graph stores relations that exist, nothing else.

A unit that is a verbatim copy of an evidence statement passes as it stands.
Pronouns ("it", "this drug") and elided subjects ("... and is phase 3") take the
subject of the clause before; with no antecedent they are reported. Text the check
cannot read (non-English letters) is reported, not skipped.

It is strict on purpose: a true fact the graph does not hold is reported as
unverifiable, and a reviewer decides. It still reads words, not meaning: a clause
whose words, entities and numbers all come from one evidence statement about
those entities, arranged into a different claim, can pass.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import re
import unicodedata

# -- vocabulary --------------------------------------------------------------- #
# relation word -> (relation, required action, voice). Required action: None means
# any edge of that relation; "inhib" / "activ" must match the edge's action type.
_R = {}
for w in ("act acts acting target targets targeting bind binds binding "
          "modulate modulates modulating").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", None, "active")
for w in ("targeted bound modulated").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", None, "passive")
for w in ("inhibit inhibits inhibiting block blocks blocking inhibitor inhibitors "
          "inhibition blocker blockers antagonist antagonists antagonises "
          "antagonizes").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "inhib", "active")
for w in ("inhibited blocked antagonised antagonized").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "inhib", "passive")
for w in ("activate activates activating activator activators agonist agonists "
          "stimulates stimulate").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "activ", "active")
for w in ("activated stimulated").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "activ", "passive")
for w in ("evaluate evaluates evaluating test tests testing study studies "
          "studying investigate investigates investigating assess assesses "
          "assessing").split():
    _R[w] = ("EVALUATES", None, "active")
for w in ("evaluated tested studied investigated assessed").split():
    _R[w] = ("EVALUATES", None, "passive")
for w in ("sponsor sponsors sponsoring run runs running conduct conducts "
          "conducting lead leads leading").split():
    _R[w] = ("SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF", None, "active")
for w in ("sponsored conducted led").split():
    _R[w] = ("SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF", None, "passive")
RELATION = _R
NOUN_FORMS = {"inhibitor", "inhibitors", "inhibition", "blocker", "blockers",
              "antagonist", "antagonists", "activator", "activators", "agonist",
              "agonists", "sponsor"}
ROLES = {"ACTS_ON": ("Drug", "Target"), "EVALUATES": ("Trial", "Drug"),
         "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF": ("Company", "Drug")}
ACTION_WORDS = {"inhib": ("inhibitor", "antagonist", "blocker", "negative"),
                "activ": ("activator", "agonist", "positive", "opener")}

NEGATION = {"not", "no", "never", "none", "neither", "nor", "without", "cannot",
            "lack", "lacks", "lacking", "absent", "fail", "fails", "failed",
            "non", "longer", "nothing", "nobody", "nowhere", "hardly", "barely",
            "unlike", "except", "instead", "rather", "stopped", "ceased"}
COMPARISON = {"more", "less", "fewer", "most", "least", "best", "worst", "better",
              "worse", "superior", "inferior", "than", "only", "first", "largest",
              "smallest", "leading", "top", "main", "major", "primary", "sole",
              "exclusively", "mainly", "mostly", "chiefly"}
HEDGE = {"may", "might", "could", "likely", "possibly", "probably", "suggest",
         "suggests", "appears", "appear", "seems", "seem", "expected", "potential",
         "potentially", "promising", "perhaps", "presumably", "reportedly",
         # modality: what a drug can, will or should do is not what an edge records
         "can", "will", "would", "should", "must", "shall"}
DEVELOP = {"develop", "develops", "developing", "developed", "developer",
           "developers", "owns", "own", "owned", "owner", "markets", "market",
           "marketed", "makes", "made", "manufactures", "manufactured", "sells",
           "sold", "commercialises", "commercializes", "originator", "invented"}

# grammar and neutral nouns that make no claim of their own
FUNCTION = set("""
a an the and or of in on at to for by with from as into within across per via
is are was were be been being has have had does do did
it its this that these those there which who whom whose what where when also
such following listed list here namely include includes including included
trial trials study studies clinical phase phases stage stages max maximum
drug drugs agent agents molecule molecules compound compounds substance substances
organisation organisations organization organizations company companies
sponsorship graph evidence record records data according source sources
retrieved known reported shown show shows mechanism mechanisms profile findings
scope edge edges among lists
""".split())
# words about the graph itself: a clause made only of these and a negation is a
# statement about what the graph can answer ("this graph cannot answer that")
META = set("""
graph question questions answer answers answered evidence retrieved scope outside
cover covers covered information available found find determine supporting support
i we this that there here
""".split())
ARTICLES = {"a", "an", "the", "its", "their", "his", "her"}
TRIAL_NOUNS = {"trial", "trials", "study", "studies"}
BE = {"is", "are", "was", "were", "be", "been", "being"}
PREDICATE = BE | {"has", "have", "had", "does", "do", "did"} | set(RELATION)
PRONOUN = {"it": None, "its": None, "they": None, "them": None, "their": None,
           "this drug": "Drug", "the drug": "Drug", "this agent": "Drug",
           "the agent": "Drug", "this molecule": "Drug", "this trial": "Trial",
           "the trial": "Trial", "this study": "Trial", "the study": "Trial",
           "this target": "Target", "the target": "Target",
           "this company": "Company", "the company": "Company",
           "the sponsor": "Company", "this sponsor": "Company"}
STEM_SUPPORT = {"approved": "approval", "approve": "approval", "approves": "approval",
                "recruits": "recruiting", "recruit": "recruiting",
                "terminates": "terminated", "completes": "completed",
                "complete": "completed"}
STATUS = ["active not recruiting", "not yet recruiting", "enrolling by invitation",
          "recruiting", "completed", "terminated", "withdrawn", "suspended",
          "unknown", "available", "no longer available", "temporarily not available"]
SCOPE_PHRASES = [r"non[\s\-]?small[\s\-]cell lung (?:cancer|carcinoma)s?", r"\bnsclc\b",
                 r"\blung (?:cancer|carcinoma)\b"]

RE_NCT = re.compile(r"\bNCT\d{8}\b", re.I)
RE_CITE = re.compile(r"\[\s*(?:(?:NCT\d{8}|CHEMBL\d+|ENSG\d+|PMID:?\s*\d+|ORG:[^\]]+)"
                     r"\s*[,;]?\s*)+\]", re.I)
RE_MDLINK = re.compile(r"\[([^\]]*)\]\([^)]*\)")
RE_PHASE = re.compile(r"\bphase\s*([0-9]+[a-z]?|i{1,3}v?|iv)\b(?:\s*/\s*([0-9]+[a-z]?|i{1,3}))?",
                      re.I)
ROMAN = {"i": "1", "ii": "2", "iii": "3", "iv": "4"}
RE_TOKEN = re.compile(r"\x00(\d+)\x00|\d+[a-z]+\b|\d[\d,]*(?:\.\d+)?%?|[a-z]+(?:_[a-z]+)*(?:'[a-z]+)?"
                      r"|n't|[,;:]")


def _flat(s: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", " ", s.lower().replace("(s)", "s")).strip()


def _squash(s: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"[\s_\-]+", "", s.lower())


def _has(label: str, low: str) -> bool:
    """Whole-word containment: "ALK" must not match "alkylating"."""
    return re.search(rf"(?<![a-z0-9]){re.escape(label.lower())}(?![a-z0-9])", low) is not None


def _non_english(s: str) -> bool:
    return any(ord(c) > 127 and unicodedata.category(c).startswith("L") for c in s)


# -- units -------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def _units(text: str) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
    """Sentences, with a list heading ("Agents acting on ALK:") joined to each item."""
    text = RE_MDLINK.sub(r"\1", text)
    text = RE_CITE.sub(" ", text)
    lines = [l.strip() for l in text.splitlines()]
    out: list[str] = []
    heading = None
    for line in lines:
        if not line:
            heading = None
            continue
        bullet = re.match(r"^(?:[-*•]|\d+[.)])\s+", line)
        body = line[bullet.end():] if bullet else line
        if bullet and heading:
            item = body.rstrip(".")
            slot = re.search(r"\b(?:these|the following|following)(?:\s+[A-Za-z]+)?",
                             heading, re.I)
            joined = (heading[:slot.start()] + item + heading[slot.end():] if slot
                      else f"{item} — {heading}")
            out.append((joined, body))
            continue
        if body.endswith(":") and not bullet:
            heading = body[:-1]
            continue
        heading = None
        # sentence ends: . ! ? followed by space, or ; outside parentheses
        depth, start = 0, 0
        for i, ch in enumerate(body):
            depth += ch == "("
            depth -= ch == ")"
            end = (ch in ".!?" and (i + 1 == len(body) or body[i + 1] == " ")
                   and not re.search(r"\b(?:vs|e\.g|i\.e|approx|no)\.?$", body[start:i], re.I))
            if end or (ch == ";" and depth <= 0):
                out.append((body[start:i + 1].strip(), body[start:i + 1].strip()))
                start = i + 1
        out.append((body[start:].strip(), body[start:].strip()))
    return [u for u in out if re.search(r"[^\W\d_]", u[0])]   # any letter, any script


# -- the check ---------------------------------------------------------------- #
def check_claims(text: str, evidence: list, linker) -> list[dict]:
    """Return one finding per unsupported claim. `linker(text)` -> entities."""
    statements = [(e.relation, (e.statement or "").lower()) for e in evidence]
    flat_statements = [_flat(s) for _, s in statements]
    findings: list[dict] = []
    last_by_type: dict[str, dict] = {}
    last_subject: dict | None = None

    for unit, bare in _units(text):
        def flag(kind, value, why, unit=unit):
            findings.append({"kind": kind, "value": value, "sentence": unit, "why": why})

        if _non_english(unit):
            flag("unreadable", unit[:40], "text in a language the check cannot read is "
                                          "not verified")
            continue
        # a verbatim copy of a whole evidence statement says exactly what it says
        if {_flat(unit), _flat(bare.rstrip("."))} & set(flat_statements):
            first = next(iter(linker(bare)), None)   # still an antecedent for "it"
            if first and first.get("type") not in ("Refused", "Disease"):
                last_subject = first
                last_by_type[first["type"]] = first
            continue

        toks, ents = _tokenise(unit, linker, last_by_type, last_subject)
        for clause in _clauses(toks):
            c_ents = [ents[t[1]] for t in clause if t[0] == "ENT"]
            gone = [e for e in c_ents if e.get("type") == "Missing"]
            words = [t[1] for t in clause if t[0] == "W"]
            if gone and len(gone) == len(c_ents) and all(
                    w in META or w in NEGATION or w in FUNCTION or w in BE
                    or w.endswith("n't") for w in words):
                continue                  # "it cannot answer this question"
            if gone:
                flag("no_antecedent", gone[0]["label"],
                     "a pronoun with nothing before it to refer to")
                continue
            pred = next((i for i, t in enumerate(clause)
                         if t[0] == "W" and t[1] in PREDICATE), len(clause))
            implicit = None
            if not any(t[0] == "ENT" for t in clause[:pred]):
                implicit = last_subject   # "... and is phase 3"
            subjects = c_ents + ([implicit] if implicit and implicit not in c_ents else [])
            if not subjects:
                words = [t[1] for t in clause if t[0] == "W"]
                content = [w for w in words if w not in FUNCTION and w not in BE]
                if content and not all(w in META or w in NEGATION or w.endswith("n't")
                                       for w in content):
                    flag("no_subject", " ".join(content[:6]),
                         "this clause names no entity the evidence can be checked against")
                continue
            labels = [e["label"].lower() for e in subjects]
            support = [s for _, s in statements if all(_has(l, s) for l in labels)]
            _check_relations(clause, toks, ents, implicit, statements, flag)
            _check_attributes(clause, ents, subjects[0], support, statements, flag)
            last_subject = c_ents[0] if c_ents else last_subject
            for e in c_ents:
                last_by_type[e["type"]] = e
    return findings


def _tokenise(unit, linker, last_by_type, last_subject):
    """Entities become placeholders, pronouns become their antecedent."""
    s = unit
    for pat in SCOPE_PHRASES:
        s = re.sub(pat, " ", s, flags=re.I)
    s = s.replace("(s)", "s").replace("’", "'")
    s = re.sub(r"[()\[\]{}\"*—–]", " ", s)
    ents: list[dict] = []
    spans = []
    for m in RE_NCT.finditer(s):
        spans.append((m.start(), m.end(), {"type": "Trial", "label": m.group(0).upper()}))
    # the linker strips only " ?.," from tokens, so "EGFR;" or "ALK:" would not link
    linked = linker(re.sub(r"[;:!]", " ", RE_NCT.sub(lambda m: " " * len(m.group(0)), s)))
    for e in linked:
        if e.get("type") in ("Disease",):
            continue
        mt = e.get("matched_text") or ""
        words = [re.escape(w) for w in mt.split()]
        if not words:
            continue
        pat = r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9])" + r"[\W_]+".join(words) + r"(?![A-Za-z0-9])"
        for m in re.finditer(pat, s, re.I):
            if not any(a < m.end() and m.start() < b for a, b, _ in spans):
                spans.append((m.start(), m.end(), e))
    # pronouns
    low = s.lower()
    for phrase, typ in sorted(PRONOUN.items(), key=lambda x: -len(x[0])):
        for m in re.finditer(rf"\b{re.escape(phrase)}\b", low):
            if any(a < m.end() and m.start() < b for a, b, _ in spans):
                continue
            after = low[m.end():]
            if re.match(r"\s+of\b", after) or any(
                    a >= m.end() and not s[m.end():a].strip(" ,") for a, _, _ in spans):
                continue                 # "the target of X", "the drug osimertinib"
            ante = last_by_type.get(typ) if typ else last_subject
            spans.append((m.start(), m.end(), ante or {"type": "Missing", "label": phrase}))
    spans.sort()
    out, last = [], 0
    for a, b, e in spans:
        out.append(s[last:a])
        out.append(f" \x00{len(ents)}\x00 ")
        ents.append(e)
        last = b
    out.append(s[last:])
    toks = []
    for m in RE_TOKEN.finditer("".join(out).lower()):
        if m.group(1) is not None:
            toks.append(("ENT", int(m.group(1))))
        elif m.group(0) in ",;:":
            toks.append(("P", m.group(0)))
        elif m.group(0)[0].isdigit():
            toks.append(("N", m.group(0).replace(",", "").rstrip("%")))
        else:
            toks.append(("W", m.group(0)))
    return toks, ents


def _clauses(toks):
    """Split at ; and at and/but/while/whereas when both sides have a predicate."""
    cuts = [-1]
    for i, t in enumerate(toks):
        if t == ("P", ";"):
            cuts.append(i)
        elif t[0] == "W" and t[1] in ("and", "but", "while", "whereas"):
            left = toks[cuts[-1] + 1:i]
            right = toks[i + 1:]
            nxt = next((j for j in range(i + 1, len(toks))
                        if toks[j][0] == "W" and toks[j][1] in ("and", "but", "while",
                                                               "whereas")
                        or toks[j] == ("P", ";")), len(toks))
            right = toks[i + 1:nxt]
            if any(x[0] == "W" and x[1] in PREDICATE for x in left) and \
                    any(x[0] == "W" and x[1] in PREDICATE for x in right):
                cuts.append(i)
    cuts.append(len(toks))
    return [toks[a + 1:b] for a, b in zip(cuts, cuts[1:]) if toks[a + 1:b]]


def _group(clause, k, step):
    """The run of entities nearest to position k in direction step, joined by , and or."""
    i = k + step
    while 0 <= i < len(clause) and clause[i][0] != "ENT":
        i += step
    grp = []
    while 0 <= i < len(clause):
        if clause[i][0] == "ENT":
            grp.append(clause[i][1])
            i += step
        elif clause[i] in (("P", ","), ("W", "and"), ("W", "or"), ("W", "as"),
                           ("W", "well")):
            i += step
        else:
            break
    return grp


def _after_group(clause, k):
    """Index just past the entity run that follows position k."""
    i = k + 1
    while i < len(clause) and clause[i][0] != "ENT":
        i += 1
    if i >= len(clause):
        return None
    while i < len(clause) and (clause[i][0] == "ENT" or clause[i] in (
            ("P", ","), ("W", "and"), ("W", "or"))):
        i += 1
    return i


def _check_relations(clause, toks, ents, implicit, statements, flag):
    for k, t in enumerate(clause):
        if t[0] != "W" or t[1] not in RELATION:
            continue
        rel, action, voice = RELATION[t[1]]
        if t[1] == "sponsor" and k and clause[k - 1] == ("W", "lead"):
            continue                       # "lead sponsor": one noun, handled at "lead"
        subj_t, obj_t = ROLES[rel]
        left = [ents[i] for i in _group(clause, k, -1)]
        right = [ents[i] for i in _group(clause, k, +1)]
        prev = clause[k - 1] if k else None
        nxt = clause[k + 1] if k + 1 < len(clause) else None
        noun = (t[1] in NOUN_FORMS or t[1] in ("target", "targets")) and (
            (prev and prev[0] == "W" and (prev[1] in ARTICLES or prev[1] in TRIAL_NOUNS))
            or nxt == ("W", "of") or nxt is None or (prev and prev[0] == "ENT"))
        if t[1] in NOUN_FORMS and prev and prev[0] == "ENT" and ents[prev[1]]["type"] == obj_t:
            right, left = [ents[prev[1]]], [ents[i] for i in _group(clause, k - 1, -1)]
        elif t[1] in ("target", "targets") and nxt == ("W", "of"):
            # "EGFR is the target of osimertinib" / "the target of osimertinib is EGFR"
            drug_side = right
            target_side = left
            if not target_side:
                after = _after_group(clause, k)
                if after is not None and after < len(clause) and clause[after][0] == "W" \
                        and clause[after][1] in BE:
                    target_side = [ents[i] for i in _group(clause, after, +1)]
            left, right = drug_side, target_side
        elif voice == "passive" and (
                any(x == ("W", b) for x in clause[max(0, k - 3):k] for b in BE)
                or nxt == ("W", "by")):
            left, right = right, left
        if not left and implicit:
            left = [implicit]
        if noun and (not left or not right):
            used = {id(e) for e in left + right}
            pool = [ents[x[1]] for x in clause if x[0] == "ENT" and id(ents[x[1]]) not in used]
            left = left or [e for e in pool if e["type"] == subj_t]
            right = right or [e for e in pool if e["type"] == obj_t]
        if rel == "EVALUATES" and not any(e["type"] == "Trial" for e in left + right):
            # without a named trial, the subject must be the word "trials" ("trials
            # evaluating X") or the drug must be the grammatical object ("X is tested")
            passive = voice == "passive" and (
                any(x == ("W", b) for x in clause[max(0, k - 3):k] for b in BE)
                or nxt == ("W", "by"))
            trial_subject = any(x[0] == "W" and x[1] in TRIAL_NOUNS
                                for x in clause[max(0, k - 3):k])
            objs = right if (passive or trial_subject) else []
            if not (passive or trial_subject):
                flag("relation_direction", f"{'/'.join(e['label'] for e in left)} {t[1]}",
                     "trials evaluate drugs; this sentence has something else evaluating")
                continue
            if not objs or any(e["type"] != "Drug" for e in objs):
                flag("relation_incomplete", t[1], "an evaluation relation whose object is "
                                                  "not a drug")
                continue
            drugs = objs
            for d in drugs:
                if not any(r in ("EVALUATES", "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF") and _has(d["label"], s)
                           for r, s in statements):
                    flag("relation", f"{d['label']} evaluated in trials",
                         "no retrieved trial evaluates this drug")
            continue
        if not left or not right:
            flag("relation_incomplete", t[1], "a relation whose two sides could not both "
                                              "be identified in the sentence")
            continue
        if any(e["type"] != subj_t for e in left) or any(e["type"] != obj_t for e in right):
            flag("relation_direction",
                 f"{'/'.join(e['label'] for e in left)} {t[1]} "
                 f"{'/'.join(e['label'] for e in right)}",
                 f"this relation runs from a {subj_t.lower()} to a {obj_t.lower()}; "
                 "the sentence has it the other way or between the wrong kinds")
            continue
        for x in left:
            for y in right:
                hits = [s for r, s in statements if r == rel and _has(x["label"], s)
                        and _has(y["label"], s)]
                if not hits:
                    flag("relation", f"{x['label']} — {t[1]} — {y['label']}",
                         "no retrieved edge links these two entities this way")
                elif action and not any(any(a in s for a in ACTION_WORDS[action])
                                        for s in hits):
                    flag("action", f"{x['label']} {t[1]} {y['label']}",
                         "the evidence gives a different or no action type")


def _owner(clause, ents, i, prefer):
    """The entity a value at position i belongs to: nearest in `prefer` direction first."""
    order = [range(i + 1, len(clause)), range(i - 1, -1, -1)]
    if prefer == "before":
        order.reverse()
    for rng in order:
        for j in rng:
            if clause[j][0] == "ENT":
                return ents[clause[j][1]]
    return None


def _check_attributes(clause, ents, subject, support, statements, flag):
    def scoped(i, prefer):
        """Evidence statements naming the clause subject and the value's own entity."""
        own = _owner(clause, ents, i, prefer) or subject
        names = {own["label"].lower(), subject["label"].lower()}
        return [s for _, s in statements if all(_has(n, s) for n in names)]

    words = [t[1] for t in clause if t[0] == "W"]
    joined = " ".join(words)
    sup_tokens: set[str] = set()
    for s in support:
        sup_tokens |= set(re.findall(r"[a-z]+(?:_[a-z]+)*", s))
    sup_flat = " " + " ".join(_flat(s) for s in support) + " "
    sup_squash = "".join(_squash(s) for s in support)

    # status phrases are one claim, in the registry's spelling
    for st in STATUS:
        if re.search(rf"\b{st}\b", joined):
            if f" {st} " not in sup_flat or (st == "recruiting" and not re.search(
                    r"(?<!not )(?<!yet )\brecruiting\b",
                    sup_flat.replace("active not recruiting", "")
                    .replace("not yet recruiting", ""))):
                flag("status", st, "no evidence statement about these entities gives "
                                   "that status")
            joined = re.sub(rf"\b{st}\b", " ", joined)
    words = joined.split()

    i = 0
    while i < len(clause):
        t = clause[i]
        if t == ("W", "phase"):
            j = i + 1
            nums = []
            while j < len(clause) and clause[j][0] in ("N", "W") and len(nums) < 2:
                v = clause[j][1]
                if clause[j][0] == "N" or v in ROMAN or re.fullmatch(r"\d+[a-z]", v):
                    nums.append(ROMAN.get(v, v))
                    j += 1
                elif v in ("or", "to"):
                    j += 1
                else:
                    break
            if nums:
                key = "phase" + "".join(nums)
                own = "".join(_squash(s) for s in scoped(i, "before"))
                if key not in own and not all(f"phase{n}" in own for n in nums):
                    flag("phase", "phase " + "/".join(nums), "no evidence statement about "
                         "these entities gives that phase")
                i = j
                continue
        if t[0] == "N":
            n = t[1]
            own = " ".join(_flat(s) for s in scoped(i, "after")).replace(",", "")
            if not re.search(rf"(?<![\d.]){re.escape(n)}(?![\d.])", own):
                flag("number", n, "no evidence statement about these entities gives "
                                  "that figure")
        if t[0] == "W" and re.fullmatch(r"\d+[a-z]", t[1]):
            flag("number", t[1], "a figure with a suffix the evidence does not use")
        i += 1

    seen = set()
    for w in words:
        if w in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(w)
        for vocab, kind, why in (
                (NEGATION, "negation", "the graph stores only relations that exist; it "
                                       "cannot confirm an absence or a failure"),
                (COMPARISON, "comparison", "a ranking or comparison is not a relation "
                                           "the graph holds"),
                (HEDGE, "speculation", "a guess about what may be true is not in the "
                                       "evidence"),
                (DEVELOP, "development_claim", "the graph records trial sponsorship, "
                                               "which is not evidence of developing, "
                                               "owning or marketing a drug")):
            if w in vocab or (kind == "negation" and (w.endswith("n't") or
                                                       w.startswith("non_"))):
                flag(kind, w, why)
                break
        else:
            if w in RELATION or w in FUNCTION or w in BE or len(w) < 2:
                continue
            if w in sup_tokens or STEM_SUPPORT.get(w) in sup_tokens:
                continue
            flag("wording", w, "this word makes a claim no evidence statement about "
                               "these entities supports")

# 주장 검사 Codex 1차 — 지적별 판정과 수리 (2026-11-03 KST)

원문 `audit_evidence_claims/r1_report.md` · 재현 스크립트 `audit_evidence_claims/independent_*.py`. 판정: CHANGES REQUIRED.
지적은 모두 이 레포에서 다시 돌려 확인했고 전부 맞았다. 단어 모음 방식으로는 고칠 수 없어 절 단위
주어–관계–목적어 읽기로 다시 썼다(`src/claims.py`).

| # | 지적 | 판정 | 수리 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 관계 방향·양쪽 대상 미확인 (`EGFR acts on osimertinib` 등 5건) | 맞음 | 관계어 위치 기준 왼쪽·오른쪽 대상 묶음, 수동태 뒤집기, 종류 불일치 = relation_direction, 한쪽 없음 = relation_incomplete |
| 2 | 대명사가 다음 문장에서 주어를 잃음 | 맞음 | 대명사·생략 주어는 앞 절의 주어로, 앞이 없으면 no_antecedent |
| 3 | 같은 문장의 다른 대상 값(단계·상태·수)을 빌려 씀 | 맞음 | 단계는 바로 앞 대상, 숫자는 바로 뒤 대상의 근거로만, 상태·말은 절의 모든 대상을 함께 말하는 근거로만 |
| 4 | quoted() 두 단어 규칙이 부정·개발 주장을 세탁 | 맞음 | 규칙 삭제. 근거 문장 전체를 그대로 옮긴 줄만 통과 |
| 5 | `non-` 부정 사라짐 | 맞음 | 폐암 이름(non-small cell)만 먼저 지우고 `non`은 부정 |
| 6 | 대괄호·한국어로 검사 우회 | 맞음 | 출처 id 대괄호만 지움, 마크다운 링크는 글자만 남김, 영어가 아닌 글은 unreadable |
| 7 | 문법 단어로만 된 임상 주장, 작용 종류 무시 | 맞음 | treatment·all·cancer 등을 문법 목록에서 뺌, inhibit/activate는 근거의 작용 종류와 대조(대상-표적 근거에 작용 종류를 넣음) |
| 8 | phase 13·3b·긴 단위·"trials: 13" | 맞음 | 단계 숫자 전체 비교, 접미사 포함, 숫자는 위치와 무관하게 주인 대상의 근거로 |
| SF | 충실한 바꿔 말하기 10/30 걸림 | 맞음 | 별칭(Tagrisso) 허용, 상태 구문, 목록 제목 합치기, 명사형(target of, inhibitor, sponsor), 수동 `sponsored by` → 1/30 (`supports trials`, 엄격 의도) |
| SF | 짝 절의 데카르트 곱 | 맞음 | 두 쪽 모두 술어가 있으면 and/but에서 절을 나눔 |
| SF | 중복 제거가 다른 문장의 지적을 숨김 | 맞음 | (값, 문장)으로 중복 제거 |
| SF | 문서가 코드보다 많이 약속 | 맞음 | README 13번, docstring, 대시보드 06, 써 보기 3번 문구를 실제 범위로 |

추가로 찾은 것: 질의 연결이 "is a"를 isa(isatuximab)로 읽던 결함(1차 전에 수리), 대명사 "the target of"를 대명사로 잘못 읽음, "EGFR;"처럼 문장부호가 붙은 이름이 연결 안 됨.
시험 128개(1차 공격 33건 전부 grounded=False, 충실한 바꿔 말하기 16건 전부 통과).

1: """Clause-level claim check for a generated answer.
2: 
3: The name-and-number check in query.py catches invented drugs and invented
4: figures. It cannot catch a false statement built from true tokens:
5: "AstraZeneca has discontinued osimertinib", "osimertinib does not act on EGFR",
6: "osimertinib is safe for all patients", "EGFR acts on osimertinib". This module
7: reads each clause the way a reviewer would: who is it about, what does it say
8: about them, and which retrieved statement says the same about the same entities?
9: 
10: A clause passes only when all of the following hold.
11: 
12:   * Its relations (acts on / inhibits / activates, evaluates, sponsors) run in the
13:     stated direction between the stated entities, and an evidence statement of that
14:     relation names both. Inhibiting or activating must match the stated action.
15:   * Its phase, status, counts and every other content word appear in an evidence
16:     statement that names every entity of the clause, so a value cannot be borrowed
17:     from another trial or drug mentioned in the same sentence or the same evidence.
18:   * It contains no negation, comparison, hedge or development/ownership claim. The
19:     graph stores relations that exist, nothing else.
20: 
21: A unit that is a verbatim copy of an evidence statement passes as it stands.
22: Pronouns ("it", "this drug") and elided subjects ("... and is phase 3") take the
23: subject of the clause before; with no antecedent they are reported. Text the check
24: cannot read (non-English letters) is reported, not skipped.
25: 
26: It is strict on purpose: a true fact the graph does not hold is reported as
27: unverifiable, and a reviewer decides. It still reads words, not meaning: a clause
28: whose words, entities and numbers all come from one evidence statement about
29: those entities, arranged into a different claim, can pass.
30: """
31: from __future__ import annotations
32: 
33: import re
34: import unicodedata
35: 
36: # -- vocabulary --------------------------------------------------------------- #
37: # relation word -> (relation, required action, voice). Required action: None means
38: # any edge of that relation; "inhib" / "activ" must match the edge's action type.
39: _R = {}
40: for w in ("act acts acting target targets targeting bind binds binding "
41:           "modulate modulates modulating").split():
42:     _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", None, "active")
43: for w in ("targeted bound modulated").split():
44:     _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", None, "passive")
45: for w in ("inhibit inhibits inhibiting block blocks blocking inhibitor inhibitors "
46:           "inhibition blocker blockers antagonist antagonists antagonises "
47:           "antagonizes").split():
48:     _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "inhib", "active")
49: for w in ("inhibited blocked antagonised antagonized").split():
50:     _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "inhib", "passive")
51: for w in ("activate activates activating activator activators agonist agonists "
52:           "stimulates stimulate").split():
53:     _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "activ", "active")
54: for w in ("activated stimulated").split():
55:     _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "activ", "passive")
56: for w in ("evaluate evaluates evaluating test tests testing study studies "
57:           "studying investigate investigates investigating assess assesses "
58:           "assessing").split():
59:     _R[w] = ("EVALUATES", None, "active")
60: for w in ("evaluated tested studied investigated assessed").split():
61:     _R[w] = ("EVALUATES", None, "passive")
62: for w in ("sponsor sponsors sponsoring run runs running conduct conducts "
63:           "conducting lead leads leading").split():
64:     _R[w] = ("SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF", None, "active")
65: for w in ("sponsored conducted led").split():
66:     _R[w] = ("SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF", None, "passive")
67: RELATION = _R
68: NOUN_FORMS = {"inhibitor", "inhibitors", "inhibition", "blocker", "blockers",
69:               "antagonist", "antagonists", "activator", "activators", "agonist",
70:               "agonists", "sponsor"}
71: ROLES = {"ACTS_ON": ("Drug", "Target"), "EVALUATES": ("Trial", "Drug"),
72:          "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF": ("Company", "Drug")}
73: ACTION_WORDS = {"inhib": ("inhibitor", "antagonist", "blocker", "negative"),
74:                 "activ": ("activator", "agonist", "positive", "opener")}
75: 
76: NEGATION = {"not", "no", "never", "none", "neither", "nor", "without", "cannot",
77:             "lack", "lacks", "lacking", "absent", "fail", "fails", "failed",
78:             "non", "longer", "nothing", "nobody", "nowhere", "hardly", "barely",
79:             "unlike", "except", "instead", "rather", "stopped", "ceased"}
80: COMPARISON = {"more", "less", "fewer", "most", "least", "best", "worst", "better",
81:               "worse", "superior", "inferior", "than", "only", "first", "largest",
82:               "smallest", "leading", "top", "main", "major", "primary", "sole",
83:               "exclusively", "mainly", "mostly", "chiefly"}
84: HEDGE = {"may", "might", "could", "likely", "possibly", "probably", "suggest",
85:          "suggests", "appears", "appear", "seems", "seem", "expected", "potential",
86:          "potentially", "promising", "perhaps", "presumably", "reportedly",
87:          # modality: what a drug can, will or should do is not what an edge records
88:          "can", "will", "would", "should", "must", "shall"}
89: DEVELOP = {"develop", "develops", "developing", "developed", "developer",
90:            "developers", "owns", "own", "owned", "owner", "markets", "market",
91:            "marketed", "makes", "made", "manufactures", "manufactured", "sells",
92:            "sold", "commercialises", "commercializes", "originator", "invented"}
93: 
94: # grammar and neutral nouns that make no claim of their own
95: FUNCTION = set("""
96: a an the and or of in on at to for by with from as into within across per via
97: is are was were be been being has have had does do did
98: it its this that these those there which who whom whose what where when also
99: such following listed list here namely include includes including included
100: trial trials study studies clinical phase phases stage stages max maximum
101: drug drugs agent agents molecule molecules compound compounds substance substances
102: organisation organisations organization organizations company companies
103: sponsorship graph evidence record records data according source sources
104: retrieved known reported shown show shows mechanism mechanisms profile findings
105: scope edge edges among lists
106: """.split())
107: # words about the graph itself: a clause made only of these and a negation is a
108: # statement about what the graph can answer ("this graph cannot answer that")
109: META = set("""
110: graph question questions answer answers answered evidence retrieved scope outside
111: cover covers covered information available found find determine supporting support
112: i we this that there here
113: """.split())
114: ARTICLES = {"a", "an", "the", "its", "their", "his", "her"}
115: TRIAL_NOUNS = {"trial", "trials", "study", "studies"}
116: BE = {"is", "are", "was", "were", "be", "been", "being"}
117: PREDICATE = BE | {"has", "have", "had", "does", "do", "did"} | set(RELATION)
118: PRONOUN = {"it": None, "its": None, "they": None, "them": None, "their": None,
119:            "this drug": "Drug", "the drug": "Drug", "this agent": "Drug",
120:            "the agent": "Drug", "this molecule": "Drug", "this trial": "Trial",
121:            "the trial": "Trial", "this study": "Trial", "the study": "Trial",
122:            "this target": "Target", "the target": "Target",
123:            "this company": "Company", "the company": "Company",
124:            "the sponsor": "Company", "this sponsor": "Company"}
125: STEM_SUPPORT = {"approved": "approval", "approve": "approval", "approves": "approval",
126:                 "recruits": "recruiting", "recruit": "recruiting",
127:                 "terminates": "terminated", "completes": "completed",
128:                 "complete": "completed"}
129: STATUS = ["active not recruiting", "not yet recruiting", "enrolling by invitation",
130:           "recruiting", "completed", "terminated", "withdrawn", "suspended",
131:           "unknown", "available", "no longer available", "temporarily not available"]
132: SCOPE_PHRASES = [r"non[\s\-]?small[\s\-]cell lung (?:cancer|carcinoma)s?", r"\bnsclc\b",
133:                  r"\blung (?:cancer|carcinoma)\b"]
134: 
135: RE_NCT = re.compile(r"\bNCT\d{8}\b", re.I)
136: RE_CITE = re.compile(r"\[\s*(?:(?:NCT\d{8}|CHEMBL\d+|ENSG\d+|PMID:?\s*\d+|ORG:[^\]]+)"
137:                      r"\s*[,;]?\s*)+\]", re.I)
138: RE_MDLINK = re.compile(r"\[([^\]]*)\]\([^)]*\)")
139: RE_PHASE = re.compile(r"\bphase\s*([0-9]+[a-z]?|i{1,3}v?|iv)\b(?:\s*/\s*([0-9]+[a-z]?|i{1,3}))?",
140:                       re.I)
141: ROMAN = {"i": "1", "ii": "2", "iii": "3", "iv": "4"}
142: RE_TOKEN = re.compile(r"\x00(\d+)\x00|\d+[a-z]+\b|\d[\d,]*(?:\.\d+)?%?|[a-z]+(?:_[a-z]+)*(?:'[a-z]+)?"
143:                       r"|n't|[,;:]")
144: 
145: 
146: def _flat(s: str) -> str:
147:     return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", " ", s.lower().replace("(s)", "s")).strip()
148: 
149: 
150: def _squash(s: str) -> str:
151:     return re.sub(r"[\s_\-]+", "", s.lower())
152: 
153: 
154: def _has(label: str, low: str) -> bool:
155:     """Whole-word containment: "ALK" must not match "alkylating"."""
156:     return re.search(rf"(?<![a-z0-9]){re.escape(label.lower())}(?![a-z0-9])", low) is not None
157: 
158: 
159: def _non_english(s: str) -> bool:
160:     return any(ord(c) > 127 and unicodedata.category(c).startswith("L") for c in s)
161: 
162: 
163: # -- units -------------------------------------------------------------------- #
164: def _units(text: str) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
165:     """Sentences, with a list heading ("Agents acting on ALK:") joined to each item."""
166:     text = RE_MDLINK.sub(r"\1", text)
167:     text = RE_CITE.sub(" ", text)
168:     lines = [l.strip() for l in text.splitlines()]
169:     out: list[str] = []
170:     heading = None
171:     for line in lines:
172:         if not line:
173:             heading = None
174:             continue
175:         bullet = re.match(r"^(?:[-*•]|\d+[.)])\s+", line)
176:         body = line[bullet.end():] if bullet else line
177:         if bullet and heading:
178:             item = body.rstrip(".")
179:             slot = re.search(r"\b(?:these|the following|following)(?:\s+[A-Za-z]+)?",
180:                              heading, re.I)
181:             joined = (heading[:slot.start()] + item + heading[slot.end():] if slot
182:                       else f"{item} — {heading}")
183:             out.append((joined, body))
184:             continue
185:         if body.endswith(":") and not bullet:
186:             heading = body[:-1]
187:             continue
188:         heading = None
189:         # sentence ends: . ! ? followed by space, or ; outside parentheses
190:         depth, start = 0, 0
191:         for i, ch in enumerate(body):
192:             depth += ch == "("
193:             depth -= ch == ")"
194:             end = (ch in ".!?" and (i + 1 == len(body) or body[i + 1] == " ")
195:                    and not re.search(r"\b(?:vs|e\.g|i\.e|approx|no)\.?$", body[start:i], re.I))
196:             if end or (ch == ";" and depth <= 0):
197:                 out.append((body[start:i + 1].strip(), body[start:i + 1].strip()))
198:                 start = i + 1
199:         out.append((body[start:].strip(), body[start:].strip()))
200:     return [u for u in out if re.search(r"[^\W\d_]", u[0])]   # any letter, any script
201: 
202: 
203: # -- the check ---------------------------------------------------------------- #
204: def check_claims(text: str, evidence: list, linker) -> list[dict]:
205:     """Return one finding per unsupported claim. `linker(text)` -> entities."""
206:     statements = [(e.relation, (e.statement or "").lower()) for e in evidence]
207:     flat_statements = [_flat(s) for _, s in statements]
208:     findings: list[dict] = []
209:     last_by_type: dict[str, dict] = {}
210:     last_subject: dict | None = None
211: 
212:     for unit, bare in _units(text):
213:         def flag(kind, value, why, unit=unit):
214:             findings.append({"kind": kind, "value": value, "sentence": unit, "why": why})
215: 
216:         if _non_english(unit):
217:             flag("unreadable", unit[:40], "text in a language the check cannot read is "
218:                                           "not verified")
219:             continue
220:         # a verbatim copy of a whole evidence statement says exactly what it says
221:         if {_flat(unit), _flat(bare.rstrip("."))} & set(flat_statements):
222:             first = next(iter(linker(bare)), None)   # still an antecedent for "it"
223:             if first and first.get("type") not in ("Refused", "Disease"):
224:                 last_subject = first
225:                 last_by_type[first["type"]] = first
226:             continue
227: 
228:         toks, ents = _tokenise(unit, linker, last_by_type, last_subject)
229:         for clause in _clauses(toks):
230:             c_ents = [ents[t[1]] for t in clause if t[0] == "ENT"]
231:             gone = [e for e in c_ents if e.get("type") == "Missing"]
232:             words = [t[1] for t in clause if t[0] == "W"]
233:             if gone and len(gone) == len(c_ents) and all(
234:                     w in META or w in NEGATION or w in FUNCTION or w in BE
235:                     or w.endswith("n't") for w in words):
236:                 continue                  # "it cannot answer this question"
237:             if gone:
238:                 flag("no_antecedent", gone[0]["label"],
239:                      "a pronoun with nothing before it to refer to")
240:                 continue
241:             pred = next((i for i, t in enumerate(clause)
242:                          if t[0] == "W" and t[1] in PREDICATE), len(clause))
243:             implicit = None
244:             if not any(t[0] == "ENT" for t in clause[:pred]):
245:                 implicit = last_subject   # "... and is phase 3"
246:             subjects = c_ents + ([implicit] if implicit and implicit not in c_ents else [])
247:             if not subjects:
248:                 words = [t[1] for t in clause if t[0] == "W"]
249:                 content = [w for w in words if w not in FUNCTION and w not in BE]
250:                 if content and not all(w in META or w in NEGATION or w.endswith("n't")
251:                                        for w in content):
252:                     flag("no_subject", " ".join(content[:6]),
253:                          "this clause names no entity the evidence can be checked against")
254:                 continue
255:             labels = [e["label"].lower() for e in subjects]
256:             support = [s for _, s in statements if all(_has(l, s) for l in labels)]
257:             _check_relations(clause, toks, ents, implicit, statements, flag)
258:             _check_attributes(clause, ents, subjects[0], support, statements, flag)
259:             last_subject = c_ents[0] if c_ents else last_subject
260:             for e in c_ents:
261:                 last_by_type[e["type"]] = e
262:     return findings
263: 
264: 
265: def _tokenise(unit, linker, last_by_type, last_subject):
266:     """Entities become placeholders, pronouns become their antecedent."""
267:     s = unit
268:     for pat in SCOPE_PHRASES:
269:         s = re.sub(pat, " ", s, flags=re.I)
270:     s = s.replace("(s)", "s").replace("’", "'")
271:     s = re.sub(r"[()\[\]{}\"*—–]", " ", s)
272:     ents: list[dict] = []
273:     spans = []
274:     for m in RE_NCT.finditer(s):
275:         spans.append((m.start(), m.end(), {"type": "Trial", "label": m.group(0).upper()}))
276:     # the linker strips only " ?.," from tokens, so "EGFR;" or "ALK:" would not link
277:     linked = linker(re.sub(r"[;:!]", " ", RE_NCT.sub(lambda m: " " * len(m.group(0)), s)))
278:     for e in linked:
279:         if e.get("type") in ("Disease",):
280:             continue
281:         mt = e.get("matched_text") or ""
282:         words = [re.escape(w) for w in mt.split()]
283:         if not words:
284:             continue
285:         pat = r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9])" + r"[\W_]+".join(words) + r"(?![A-Za-z0-9])"
286:         for m in re.finditer(pat, s, re.I):
287:             if not any(a < m.end() and m.start() < b for a, b, _ in spans):
288:                 spans.append((m.start(), m.end(), e))
289:     # pronouns
290:     low = s.lower()
1: """A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.
2: 
3:     python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765
4: 
5: Three boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
6: how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
7: evidence, or paste an answer (say, one a language model wrote) and see what the
8: groundedness check flags against the evidence for that question. All three call
9: the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
10: OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
11: pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
12: """
13: from __future__ import annotations
14: 
15: import json
16: import sqlite3
17: import sys
18: import threading
19: from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
20: from pathlib import Path
21: 
22: sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
23: import config as C
24: import normalize as N
25: from query import OncoKG
26: 
27: PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
28: PORT = 8765
29: 
30: 
31: class Engine:
32:     def __init__(self):
33:         self.kg = OncoKG()
34:         # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
35:         # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
36:         self.kg.conn.close()
37:         self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
38:         self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
39:         self.lock = threading.Lock()
40:         self.dic = self.kg.dic
41:         self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
42:         target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
43:         self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)
44: 
45:     def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
46:         out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
47:         for c in out["components"]:
48:             d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
49:             if d:
50:                 c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
51:                              ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
52:                 c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
53:                                            for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
54:                 c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
55:                     "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
56:                     (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
57:                 par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
58:                 if par:
59:                     c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
60:                         "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
61:                         (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
62:             if c.get("candidates"):
63:                 c["candidate_names"] = [
64:                     (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
65:         return out
66: 
67:     def ask(self, question: str) -> dict:
68:         return self.kg.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()
69: 
70:     def check(self, question: str, answer: str) -> dict:
71:         """Check an answer someone else wrote against the evidence for the question."""
72:         kg = self.kg
73:         entities = kg.link_entities(question)
74:         intent = kg.classify(question, entities)
75:         evidence = kg.retrieve(intent, entities)
76:         checks = kg.check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, generated=True,
77:                                        vocabulary=set(self.dic["index"]),
78:                                        linker=kg.link_entities)
79:         return {"intent": intent, "entities": entities, "checks": checks,
80:                 "evidence": [{"statement": e.statement, "source_record": e.source_record,
81:                               "source": e.source, "url": e.url} for e in evidence[:8]],
82:                 "evidence_count": len(evidence)}
83: 
84: 
85: def make_handler(engine: Engine):
86:     class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
87:         def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype: str):
88:             self.send_response(code)
89:             self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
90:             self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
91:             self.end_headers()
92:             self.wfile.write(body)
93: 
94:         def do_GET(self):
95:             if self.path in ("/", "/index.html"):
96:                 self._send(200, PAGE.read_bytes(), "text/html; charset=utf-8")
97:             else:
98:                 self._send(404, b"not found", "text/plain")
99: 
100:         def do_POST(self):
101:             try:
102:                 n = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
103:                 body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(min(n, 20000)) or b"{}")
104:                 text = str(body.get("text") or "").strip()[:500]
105:                 if not text:
106:                     raise ValueError("empty input")
107:                 if self.path not in ("/api/resolve", "/api/ask", "/api/check"):
108:                     return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
109:                 with engine.lock:
110:                     if self.path == "/api/resolve":
111:                         res = engine.resolve(text)
112:                     elif self.path == "/api/ask":
113:                         res = engine.ask(text)
114:                     else:
115:                         answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
116:                         if not answer:
117:                             raise ValueError("empty answer")
118:                         res = engine.check(text, answer)
119:                 self._send(200, json.dumps(res, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
120:                            "application/json; charset=utf-8")
121:             except Exception as e:  # show the reason on the page, not a dead request
122:                 self._send(400, json.dumps({"error": str(e)}, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
123:                            "application/json; charset=utf-8")
124: 
125:         def log_message(self, *a):
126:             pass
127:     return Handler
128: 
129: 
130: def main() -> None:
131:     port = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else PORT
132:     engine = Engine()
133:     print(f"OncoKG try-it page: http://localhost:{port}", flush=True)
134:     ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", port), make_handler(engine)).serve_forever()

138:     main()

92: class OncoKG:
93:     def __init__(self, db_path: Path = DB_PATH,
94:                  resolved_path: Path = C.PROCESSED / "resolved.json"):
95:         self.conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)
96:         self.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
97:         resolved = json.loads(resolved_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
98:         self.dic = resolved["dictionary"]
99:         self.index_keys = list(self.dic["index"].keys())
100:         self.disease = resolved["disease"]
101:         self.targets = {t["symbol"].lower(): t for t in resolved["targets"]
102:                         if t.get("symbol")}
103:         # one name can denote several organisations (a group and a subsidiary
104:         # renamed after it), so a key maps to every matching node, not one.
105:         self.companies: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
106:         for row in self.conn.execute(
107:                 "SELECT id,label FROM node WHERE type='Company' AND retired_at IS NULL"):
108:             self.companies.setdefault(T.canonical_key(row["label"]), []).append(row["id"])
109:             self.companies.setdefault(row["id"].removeprefix("ORG:"), []).append(row["id"])
110:         # a former name ("BeiGene") must still find the merged node
111:         for old, same in N.CURATED_SAME_ENTITY.items():
112:             if same in self.companies:
113:                 self.companies.setdefault(old, []).extend(self.companies[same])
114:         self.companies = {k: sorted(set(v)) for k, v in self.companies.items()}
115: 
116:     # -- entity linking ---------------------------------------------------- #
117:     def link_entities(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
118:         found: list[dict] = []
119:         seen: set = set()
120:         tokens = [t.strip(" ?.,") for t in re.split(r"[\s,]+", question) if t.strip(" ?.,")]
121: 
122:         # The resolver's refusals apply to questions too. Linking straight
123:         # against the index let "Mvasi" or "liposomal doxorubicin" answer as
124:         # the originator molecule. A token naming a distinct product is
125:         # refused; a qualifier ("liposomal", "biosimilar") takes its
126:         # neighbours with it.
127:         refused: set[int] = set()
128:         for i, tok in enumerate(tokens):
129:             if T.is_distinct_product(tok) or T.is_radiotracer(tok):
130:                 refused.add(i)
131:                 if not T.canonical_key(tok) in self.dic["index"]:
132:                     refused.update({i - 1, i + 1})
133:         refused = {x for x in refused if 0 <= x < len(tokens)}
134:         run: list[int] = []
135:         for i in sorted(refused) + [None]:
136:             if run and (i is None or i != run[-1] + 1):
137:                 span = " ".join(tokens[run[0]:run[-1] + 1])
138:                 found.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": span,
139:                               "matched_text": span,
140:                               "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not "
141:                                      "the originator molecule"})
142:                 run = []
143:             if i is not None:
144:                 run.append(i)
145: 
146:         for size in range(min(5, len(tokens)), 0, -1):
147:             for i in range(len(tokens) - size + 1):
148:                 if refused & set(range(i, i + size)):
149:                     continue
150:                 window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
151:                 if len(window) < 3:
152:                     continue
153:                 key = T.canonical_key(window)
154: 
155:                 hits = self.dic["index"].get(key)
156:                 # Several words only make a drug name when the name itself has
157:                 # several words. The key drops spaces, so "is a" read as the
158:                 # abbreviation "isa" (isatuximab) in any question or answer.
159:                 if hits and size > 1 and not re.search(r"[\s\-/]", hits[0]["alias"]):
160:                     hits = None
161:                 if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
162:                     did = hits[0]["drug_id"]
163:                     if ("Drug", did) not in seen:
164:                         seen.add(("Drug", did))
165:                         found.append({"type": "Drug", "id": did,
166:                                       "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
167:                                       "matched_text": window,
168:                                       "matched_alias": hits[0]["alias"]})
169:                     continue
170: 
171:                 if window.lower() in self.targets:
172:                     t = self.targets[window.lower()]
173:                     if ("Target", t["target_id"]) not in seen:
174:                         seen.add(("Target", t["target_id"]))
175:                         found.append({"type": "Target", "id": t["target_id"],
176:                                       "label": t["symbol"], "matched_text": window})
177:                     continue
178: 
179:                 if key in self.companies:
180:                     cid = self.companies[key][0]
181:                     if ("Company", cid) not in seen:
182:                         seen.add(("Company", cid))
183:                         row = self.conn.execute(
184:                             "SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=?", (cid,)).fetchone()
185:                         found.append({"type": "Company", "id": cid,
186:                                       "label": row["label"], "matched_text": window,
187:                                       "also_matching_ids": self.companies[key][1:]})
188:                     continue
189: 
190:                 if key in self.disease["index"]:
191:                     if ("Disease", self.disease["disease_id"]) not in seen:
192:                         seen.add(("Disease", self.disease["disease_id"]))
193:                         found.append({"type": "Disease",
194:                                       "id": self.disease["disease_id"],
195:                                       "label": self.disease["name"],
438:             model="claude-sonnet-4-5", max_tokens=700,
439:             messages=[{"role": "user", "content": prompt}])
440:         return msg.content[0].text, "language model, constrained to retrieved evidence"
441: 
442:     # -- answer quality ---------------------------------------------------- #
443:     @staticmethod
444:     def check_groundedness(text: str, evidence: list[Evidence],
445:                            entities: list[dict], generated: bool = True,
446:                            vocabulary: set | None = None, linker=None) -> dict:
447:         """Verify a *generated* answer says nothing the evidence does not contain.
448: 
449:         Two things this check is not, both found by an adversarial review and
450:         written down here rather than papered over:
451: 
452:         1. It is meaningless on the template answer. The template is built by
453:            concatenating the evidence statements, so checking it against those
454:            same statements is circular -- it cannot fail, and reporting
455:            `grounded=True` for it would be a test that always passes. When the
456:            answer was rendered rather than generated, the check reports
457:            `applicable=False` and asserts nothing.
458:         2. Names and numbers alone are a floor. It verifies that capitalised
459:            entities, drug-shaped names and numbers appear in the retrieved set,
460:            and refuses figures written in words. Given a `linker` (the query
461:            layer's entity linker), it also reads the answer sentence by sentence
462:            (claims.check_claims): every stated relation must hold between the
463:            same entities in the evidence, and every other content word must be
464:            grammar or appear in evidence about that sentence's own entities, so
465:            "AstraZeneca has discontinued this drug", "does not act on EGFR" and
466:            "is safe" are reported. It reads words, not meaning; see claims.py.
467:         """
468:         if not generated:
469:             return {
470:                 "grounded": None,
471:                 "applicable": False,
472:                 "unsupported_claims": [],
473:                 "evidence_count": len(evidence),
474:                 "method": "not applicable",
475:                 "why": ("the answer was rendered directly from the retrieved "
476:                         "evidence, so checking it against that evidence is "
477:                         "circular and would always pass"),
478:             }
479: 
480:         haystack = " ".join(
481:             [e.statement for e in evidence]
482:             + [str(v) for e in evidence for v in e.detail.values()]
483:             + [e.source_record or "" for e in evidence]
484:             + [en["label"] for en in entities]
485:         ).lower()
486:         # Words are tokenised so "47" is not satisfied by NCT02303977. Numbers
487:         # are tokenised separately and whole, decimals included: splitting on
488:         # non-alphanumerics turned evidence "47.5" into the tokens "47" and
489:         # "5", so an invented "47" passed and the exact "47.5" was rejected.
490:         haystack_tokens = set(re.findall(r"[a-z0-9]+", haystack))
491:         haystack_numbers = {n.replace(",", "") for n in RE_NUMBER.findall(haystack)}
492: 
493:         unsupported: list[dict] = []
494: 
495:         def alias_in_evidence(word: str) -> bool:
496:             if linker is None:
497:                 return False
498:             return any(e.get("type") in ("Drug", "Target", "Company")
499:                        and e["label"].lower() in haystack for e in linker(word))
500: 
501:         # "phase 3" is stated as PHASE3 in the evidence, so a phase number is
502:         # matched against that form, not against the bare figures
503:         haystack_flat = re.sub(r"[\s_\-]+", "", haystack)
504:         phase_nums = {n for m in re.finditer(r"\bphase\s*(\d)(?:\s*/\s*(\d))?\b", text, re.I)
505:                       for n in m.groups() if n}
506:         for num in set(RE_NUMBER.findall(text)):
507:             if num in phase_nums and (linker is not None or f"phase{num}" in haystack_flat):
508:                 continue      # with a linker, claims.py checks the phase per entity
509:             if num.replace(",", "") not in haystack_numbers:
510:                 unsupported.append({"kind": "number", "value": num,
511:                                     "why": "not present in the retrieved evidence"})
512: 
513:         # A figure written in words cannot be matched against the evidence, so
514:         # it is reported as unverifiable rather than allowed through.
515:         for word in set(RE_NUMBER_WORD.findall(text.lower())):
516:             unsupported.append({"kind": "number_in_words", "value": word,
517:                                 "why": "a figure written in words cannot be "
518:                                        "checked against the evidence"})
519: 
520:         capitalised = set(re.findall(r"\b[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9\-]{3,}\b", text))
521:         stop = {"Scope", "Organisations", "Trials", "Profile", "Findings",
522:                 "Agents", "Reported", "This", "The", "None", "NSCLC", "What",
523:                 "There", "These", "That", "Those", "According", "Sponsors"}
524:         for name in capitalised - stop:
525:             if name.lower() not in haystack and not alias_in_evidence(name):
526:                 unsupported.append({"kind": "entity", "value": name,
527:                                     "why": "named in the answer but not in the evidence"})
528: 
529:         # Drug names are usually lowercase in prose ("xyzabumab cures cancer"
530:         # passed the capitalised-only check). Anything shaped like an INN or a
531:         # development code must appear in the evidence too.
532:         for name in set(RE_DRUGLIKE.findall(text)):
533:             if (name.lower() not in haystack_tokens and name.lower() not in haystack
534:                     and not alias_in_evidence(name)):
535:                 unsupported.append({"kind": "drug_like_name", "value": name,
536:                                     "why": "looks like a drug name or code but "
537:                                            "is not in the evidence"})
538: 
539:         # Any word the drug vocabulary knows ("aspirin") is a drug name,
540:         # whatever its shape, and must be in the evidence.
541:         if vocabulary:
542:             for w in set(re.findall(r"[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9\-]{3,}", text)):
543:                 if (T.canonical_key(w) in vocabulary and w.lower() not in haystack
544:                         and not alias_in_evidence(w)):
545:                     unsupported.append({"kind": "drug_name", "value": w,
546:                                         "why": "a known drug name not in the evidence"})
547: 
548:         # Figures in forms the number matcher does not read: scientific
549:         # notation ("1e6") and Unicode fractions or superscripts ("⅔").
550:         for odd in set(RE_ODD_NUMBER.findall(text)):
551:             unsupported.append({"kind": "number_unreadable", "value": odd,
552:                                 "why": "a figure in a form the check cannot match"})
553: 
554:         if linker is not None:
555:             from claims import check_claims
556:             unsupported.extend(check_claims(text, evidence, linker))
557: 
558:         # one finding per value, first reason kept, in a stable order
559:         seen_values: set[str] = set()
560:         key = lambda u: (str(u["value"]), u.get("sentence", ""))
561:         unsupported = [u for u in sorted(unsupported, key=lambda u: (*key(u), u["kind"]))
562:                        if not (key(u) in seen_values or seen_values.add(key(u)))]
563: 
564:         return {
565:             "grounded": not unsupported,
566:             "applicable": True,
567:             "unsupported_claims": unsupported,
568:             "evidence_count": len(evidence),
569:             "method": ("entity and tokenised-numeric overlap against the "
570:                        "retrieved evidence" + ("; sentence claims checked against "
"""Read-only reproduction for the independent audit of 0c441e0.

Run: python -B audit_evidence_claims/independent_main.py
Only writes the adjacent JSON evidence file.

import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path
