from app import Engine
from claims import check_claims, _units

engine = Engine()
Q_TARGET = "What does osimertinib target?"
Q_SPONSOR = "Which companies are developing osimertinib?"
Q_TRIAL = "What trials evaluate Tagrisso?"
Q_ALK = "Which drugs act on ALK?"
Q_PROFILE = "What is osimertinib?"

CASES = [
    ("control_safe", Q_TARGET, "Osimertinib is safe."),
    ("control_discontinued", Q_SPONSOR, "AstraZeneca has discontinued osimertinib."),

    ("clinical_from_alk", Q_ALK, "PLB1003 is approved for all lung cancer and acts on ALK."),
    ("modality_can", Q_TARGET, "Osimertinib can act on EGFR."),
    ("modality_will", Q_TARGET, "Osimertinib will act on EGFR."),

    ("agonist_as_blocks", "What does imiquimod target?", "Imiquimod blocks TLR7."),

    ("punctuation_only", Q_TARGET, "..."),
]

results = []
for case_id, question, answer in CASES:
    result = engine.check(question, answer)
    ents = engine.kg.link_entities(question)
    ev = engine.kg.retrieve(engine.kg.classify(question, ents), ents)
    raw = check_claims(answer, ev, engine.kg.link_entities)
    row = {"id": case_id, "question": question, "answer": answer,
           "result": result, "raw_claim_findings": raw,
           "units": _units(answer), "answer_entities": engine.kg.link_entities(answer)}
    results.append(row)
    print(json.dumps({"id": case_id, "grounded": result["checks"]["grounded"],
                      "findings": [(u["kind"], u["value"]) for u in result["checks"]["unsupported_claims"]]},
                     ensure_ascii=True))

out = Path(__file__).with_name("independent_main_results.json")
out.write_text(json.dumps(results, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")

361: def _after_group(clause, k):
362:     """Index just past the entity run that follows position k."""
363:     i = k + 1
364:     while i < len(clause) and clause[i][0] != "ENT":
365:         i += 1
366:     if i >= len(clause):
367:         return None
368:     while i < len(clause) and (clause[i][0] == "ENT" or clause[i] in (
369:             ("P", ","), ("W", "and"), ("W", "or"))):
370:         i += 1
371:     return i
372: 
373: 
374: def _check_relations(clause, toks, ents, implicit, statements, flag):
375:     for k, t in enumerate(clause):
376:         if t[0] != "W" or t[1] not in RELATION:
377:             continue
378:         rel, action, voice = RELATION[t[1]]
379:         if t[1] == "sponsor" and k and clause[k - 1] == ("W", "lead"):
380:             continue                       # "lead sponsor": one noun, handled at "lead"
381:         subj_t, obj_t = ROLES[rel]
382:         left = [ents[i] for i in _group(clause, k, -1)]
383:         right = [ents[i] for i in _group(clause, k, +1)]
384:         prev = clause[k - 1] if k else None
385:         nxt = clause[k + 1] if k + 1 < len(clause) else None
386:         noun = (t[1] in NOUN_FORMS or t[1] in ("target", "targets")) and (
387:             (prev and prev[0] == "W" and (prev[1] in ARTICLES or prev[1] in TRIAL_NOUNS))
388:             or nxt == ("W", "of") or nxt is None or (prev and prev[0] == "ENT"))
389:         if t[1] in NOUN_FORMS and prev and prev[0] == "ENT" and ents[prev[1]]["type"] == obj_t:
390:             right, left = [ents[prev[1]]], [ents[i] for i in _group(clause, k - 1, -1)]
391:         elif t[1] in ("target", "targets") and nxt == ("W", "of"):
392:             # "EGFR is the target of osimertinib" / "the target of osimertinib is EGFR"
393:             drug_side = right
394:             target_side = left
395:             if not target_side:
396:                 after = _after_group(clause, k)
397:                 if after is not None and after < len(clause) and clause[after][0] == "W" \
398:                         and clause[after][1] in BE:
399:                     target_side = [ents[i] for i in _group(clause, after, +1)]
400:             left, right = drug_side, target_side
401:         elif voice == "passive" and (
402:                 any(x == ("W", b) for x in clause[max(0, k - 3):k] for b in BE)
403:                 or nxt == ("W", "by")):
404:             left, right = right, left
405:         if not left and implicit:
406:             left = [implicit]
407:         if noun and (not left or not right):
408:             used = {id(e) for e in left + right}
409:             pool = [ents[x[1]] for x in clause if x[0] == "ENT" and id(ents[x[1]]) not in used]
410:             left = left or [e for e in pool if e["type"] == subj_t]
411:             right = right or [e for e in pool if e["type"] == obj_t]
412:         if rel == "EVALUATES" and not any(e["type"] == "Trial" for e in left + right):
413:             # without a named trial, the subject must be the word "trials" ("trials
414:             # evaluating X") or the drug must be the grammatical object ("X is tested")
415:             passive = voice == "passive" and (
416:                 any(x == ("W", b) for x in clause[max(0, k - 3):k] for b in BE)
417:                 or nxt == ("W", "by"))
418:             trial_subject = any(x[0] == "W" and x[1] in TRIAL_NOUNS
419:                                 for x in clause[max(0, k - 3):k])
420:             objs = right if (passive or trial_subject) else []
421:             if not (passive or trial_subject):
422:                 flag("relation_direction", f"{'/'.join(e['label'] for e in left)} {t[1]}",
423:                      "trials evaluate drugs; this sentence has something else evaluating")
424:                 continue
425:             if not objs or any(e["type"] != "Drug" for e in objs):
426:                 flag("relation_incomplete", t[1], "an evaluation relation whose object is "
427:                                                   "not a drug")
428:                 continue
429:             drugs = objs
430:             for d in drugs:
431:                 if not any(r in ("EVALUATES", "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF") and _has(d["label"], s)
432:                            for r, s in statements):
433:                     flag("relation", f"{d['label']} evaluated in trials",
434:                          "no retrieved trial evaluates this drug")
435:             continue
436:         if not left or not right:
437:             flag("relation_incomplete", t[1], "a relation whose two sides could not both "
438:                                               "be identified in the sentence")
439:             continue
440:         if any(e["type"] != subj_t for e in left) or any(e["type"] != obj_t for e in right):
441:             flag("relation_direction",
442:                  f"{'/'.join(e['label'] for e in left)} {t[1]} "
443:                  f"{'/'.join(e['label'] for e in right)}",
444:                  f"this relation runs from a {subj_t.lower()} to a {obj_t.lower()}; "
445:                  "the sentence has it the other way or between the wrong kinds")
446:             continue
447:         for x in left:
448:             for y in right:
449:                 hits = [s for r, s in statements if r == rel and _has(x["label"], s)
450:                         and _has(y["label"], s)]
451:                 if not hits:
452:                     flag("relation", f"{x['label']} — {t[1]} — {y['label']}",
453:                          "no retrieved edge links these two entities this way")
454:                 elif action and not any(any(a in s for a in ACTION_WORDS[action])
455:                                         for s in hits):
456:                     flag("action", f"{x['label']} {t[1]} {y['label']}",
457:                          "the evidence gives a different or no action type")
458: 
459: 
460: def _owner(clause, ents, i, prefer):
461:     """The entity a value at position i belongs to: nearest in `prefer` direction first."""
462:     order = [range(i + 1, len(clause)), range(i - 1, -1, -1)]
463:     if prefer == "before":
464:         order.reverse()
465:     for rng in order:
466:         for j in rng:
467:             if clause[j][0] == "ENT":
468:                 return ents[clause[j][1]]
469:     return None
470: 
471: 
472: def _check_attributes(clause, ents, subject, support, statements, flag):
473:     def scoped(i, prefer):
474:         """Evidence statements naming the clause subject and the value's own entity."""
475:         own = _owner(clause, ents, i, prefer) or subject
476:         names = {own["label"].lower(), subject["label"].lower()}
477:         return [s for _, s in statements if all(_has(n, s) for n in names)]
478: 
479:     words = [t[1] for t in clause if t[0] == "W"]
480:     joined = " ".join(words)
481:     sup_tokens: set[str] = set()
482:     for s in support:
483:         sup_tokens |= set(re.findall(r"[a-z]+(?:_[a-z]+)*", s))
484:     sup_flat = " " + " ".join(_flat(s) for s in support) + " "
485:     sup_squash = "".join(_squash(s) for s in support)
486: 
487:     # status phrases are one claim, in the registry's spelling
488:     for st in STATUS:
489:         if re.search(rf"\b{st}\b", joined):
490:             if f" {st} " not in sup_flat or (st == "recruiting" and not re.search(
491:                     r"(?<!not )(?<!yet )\brecruiting\b",
492:                     sup_flat.replace("active not recruiting", "")
493:                     .replace("not yet recruiting", ""))):

495:                                    "that status")
496:             joined = re.sub(rf"\b{st}\b", " ", joined)
497:     words = joined.split()
498: 
499:     i = 0
500:     while i < len(clause):
501:         t = clause[i]
502:         if t == ("W", "phase"):
503:             j = i + 1
504:             nums = []
505:             while j < len(clause) and clause[j][0] in ("N", "W") and len(nums) < 2:
506:                 v = clause[j][1]
507:                 if clause[j][0] == "N" or v in ROMAN or re.fullmatch(r"\d+[a-z]", v):
508:                     nums.append(ROMAN.get(v, v))
509:                     j += 1
510:                 elif v in ("or", "to"):
511:                     j += 1
512:                 else:
513:                     break
514:             if nums:
515:                 key = "phase" + "".join(nums)
516:                 own = "".join(_squash(s) for s in scoped(i, "before"))
517:                 if key not in own and not all(f"phase{n}" in own for n in nums):
518:                     flag("phase", "phase " + "/".join(nums), "no evidence statement about "
519:                          "these entities gives that phase")

521:                 continue
522:         if t[0] == "N":

530:         i += 1

532:     seen = set()

537:         for vocab, kind, why in (

539:                                        "cannot confirm an absence or a failure"),

545:                                                "which is not evidence of developing, "

# Standards and documentation axis

No separate repository coding-standard source was found. No tracked file was edited; these are behavioral/documentation findings, not invented style violations. Reproduce: `python -B audit_evidence_claims/r2/standards_probe.py`; exact inputs, outputs, linker spans and evidence are in `standards_probe.json`.

## BLOCKERS

**Refused product spans bypass the checker.** Question: `What does osimertinib target?`; sole evidence: `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`. Each answer returns `grounded=True, unsupported_claims=[]`:

- `osimertinib is liposomal.`
- `osimertinib is a biosimilar.`
- `Osimertinib is not liposomal.`
- `liposomal Tagrisso.`
- `Osimertinib is non-liposomal.`

`src/query.py:128-141` deliberately returns `Refused` spans including adjacent words. `src/claims.py:278-288` turns them into entity placeholders, removing their content (including negation); `src/claims.py:231-240` rejects only `Missing`, not `Refused`. Empty wording checks then pass. The alias exemption (`src/query.py:495-499,543-544`) also admits Tagrisso individually although its enclosing product was refused. This violates the requested unsupported ordinary-word/negation detection.

## SHOULD FIX

1. Faithful aliases remain rejected for the same question: `Osimertinib (Tagrisso) acts on EGFR.` returns False, `wording: tagrisso`; `AZD 9291 acts on EGFR.` returns False, `number: 9291`. Entity dedup loses subsequent alias spans (`src/query.py:163-168`; `src/claims.py:281-288`); numeric overlap does not exempt linked code spans (`src/query.py:506-511`). `Tagrisso acts on EGFR.` and `AZD9291 acts on EGFR.` pass.
2. Documentation still overstates: README.md:508-520 / README.ko.md:354-360 say negations are always reported and describe a narrower residual bypass. Refused-span examples contradict this. Exact sponsorship evidence containing `not evidence of developing the drug` passes; `This graph cannot answer that question.` passes for the France question. Intended exceptions should be stated. `src/query.py:447,461-466`, `web/template.html:263-265`, and `web/try.html:69-71` retain categorical guarantees. `claims.py:21` does disclose verbatim exemption; `web/try.html:142` is improved but still overstates matched values.

## NOTES

Question punctuation remains narrower than claim punctuation: `Which drugs act on ALK!` (also `:` / `;`) returns `intent=unsupported, entities=[]`; `?` works (`query.py:120` versus `claims.py:277`). Of 2,634 stored multiword aliases, 2,566 link their expected ID; five lose it during claim tokenization: two parenthesized chemical names, three overlapping parent/salt aliases. This is a parser discrepancy, not a regression in the original linker gate.

"""Re-run the six R1 probes without writing outside the R2 scratch directory."""
import difflib
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess
import sys

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
TEMP = OUT / "replay_temp"
TEMP.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
env = dict(os.environ, PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE="1", PYTHONIOENCODING="utf-8",
           TEMP=str(TEMP), TMP=str(TEMP))
names = ["main", "adversarial_probe", "adversarial_pairs", "faithful_probe",
         "code_dictionary", "code_probes"]
manifest = []
for name in names:
    source = ROOT / "audit_evidence_claims" / f"independent_{name}.py"
    dest = OUT / f"replay_{name}.py"
    original = source.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    adapted = original.replace(".parents[1]", ".parents[2]")
    adapted = adapted.replace("audit_evidence_claims/independent_", "audit_evidence_claims/r2/replay_")
    adapted = adapted.replace("independent_", "replay_")
    dest.write_text(adapted, encoding="utf-8")
    diff = "".join(difflib.unified_diff(original.splitlines(True), adapted.splitlines(True),
                                     fromfile=source.name, tofile=dest.name))
    (OUT / f"replay_{name}_adaptation.diff").write_text(diff, encoding="utf-8")
    proc = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", str(dest)], cwd=ROOT, env=env,
                          capture_output=True, text=True, encoding="utf-8")
    (OUT / f"replay_{name}.stdout.txt").write_text(proc.stdout, encoding="utf-8")
    (OUT / f"replay_{name}.stderr.txt").write_text(proc.stderr, encoding="utf-8")
    manifest.append({"source":str(source.relative_to(ROOT)), "source_sha256":hashlib.sha256(source.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),
                     "copy":str(dest.relative_to(ROOT)), "returncode":proc.returncode})
    print(name, proc.returncode, flush=True)
proc = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "pytest", "tests", "-q", "-p", "no:cacheprovider"],
                      cwd=ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True, text=True, encoding="utf-8")
(OUT / "replay_pytest.stdout.txt").write_text(proc.stdout, encoding="utf-8")
(OUT / "replay_pytest.stderr.txt").write_text(proc.stderr, encoding="utf-8")
manifest.append({"command":"python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider", "returncode":proc.returncode})
(OUT / "replay_manifest.json").write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
print(proc.stdout, flush=True)
print(proc.stderr, flush=True)
sys.exit(any(x["returncode"] for x in manifest))

import json, sys
from pathlib import Path
from dataclasses import asdict
sys.dont_write_bytecode = True
ROOT=Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
sys.path.insert(0,str(ROOT/'src'))
from app import Engine
from claims import _units, _tokenise, _clauses
e=Engine(); kg=e.kg
Q2='What trials evaluate Tagrisso?'
Q3='What does osimertinib target?'
Q4='Which drugs act on ALK?'
Q5='What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?'
QT=[Q3,'What does crizotinib target?']
rows=[]
def run(ident,qs,answer):
    if isinstance(qs,str): qs=[qs]
    entities=[]; ev=[]
    for q in qs:
        en=kg.link_entities(q); entities.extend(en)
        ev.extend(kg.retrieve(kg.classify(q,en),en))
    r=kg.check_groundedness(answer,ev,entities,True,set(kg.dic['index']),linker=kg.link_entities)
    rows.append(dict(id=ident,questions=qs,answer=answer,checks=r,evidence=[asdict(x) for x in ev],units=_units(answer)))
    print(ident,r['grounded'],[(f['kind'],f['value']) for f in r['unsupported_claims']])

"""Read-only graph probes: refused spans, alias acceptance, punctuation, docs.

Run from repository root: python -B audit_evidence_claims/r2/standards_probe.py
All writes are next to this file; no network or model calls.
"""
import sys
import json
import re
from pathlib import Path

sys.dont_write_bytecode = True
ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "src"))
from app import Engine
from claims import _tokenise

engine = Engine()
q = "What does osimertinib target?"
cases = [
    ("refused_liposomal", q, "osimertinib is liposomal."),
    ("refused_biosimilar", q, "osimertinib is a biosimilar."),
    ("refused_negation", q, "Osimertinib is not liposomal."),
    ("refused_alias", q, "liposomal Tagrisso."),
    ("refused_non", q, "Osimertinib is non-liposomal."),
    ("control_ordinary", q, "Osimertinib is safe."),
    ("control_alias", q, "Tagrisso acts on EGFR."),
    ("alias_parentheses", q, "Osimertinib (Tagrisso) acts on EGFR."),
    ("alias_spaced_code", q, "AZD 9291 acts on EGFR."),
    ("alias_unspaced_code", q, "AZD9291 acts on EGFR."),
    ("punctuation_claim", q, "Osimertinib acts on EGFR!"),
    ("refusal", "What is the capital of France?", "This graph cannot answer that question."),
]
company_q = "Which companies are developing osimertinib?"
cases.append(("verbatim_exception", company_q,
              engine.ask(company_q)["evidence"][0]["statement"]))
results = []
for name, question, answer in cases:
    result = engine.check(question, answer)
    toks, ents = _tokenise(answer, engine.kg.link_entities, {}, None)
    results.append({"case": name, "question": question, "answer": answer,
                    "linked": engine.kg.link_entities(answer),
                    "tokens": toks, "token_entities": ents, **result})

punctuation = []
for suffix in ("?", "!", ":", ";", ",", "."):
    question = "Which drugs act on ALK" + suffix
    result = engine.ask(question)
    punctuation.append({"question": question, "intent": result["intent"],
                        "entities": result["entities"], "answer": result["answer"],
                        "evidence_count": len(result["evidence"])})

alias_rows = []
for hits in engine.dic["index"].values():
    for hit in hits:
        if re.search(r"\s", hit["alias"]):
            alias_rows.append((hit["alias"], hit["drug_id"]))
aliases = sorted({a for a, _ in alias_rows})
losses = []
total_linked = 0
for alias in aliases:
    linked = engine.kg.link_entities(alias)
    before = {e["id"] for e in linked if e["type"] == "Drug"}
    expected = {d for a, d in alias_rows if a == alias}
    intended = before & expected
    if not intended:
        continue
    total_linked += 1
    _, entities = _tokenise(alias, engine.kg.link_entities, {}, None)
    after = {e.get("id") for e in entities if e["type"] == "Drug"}
    if not intended & after:
        losses.append({"alias": alias, "linked": linked, "token_entities": entities})

output = {"cases": results, "punctuation_questions": punctuation,
          "alias_tokenisation": {"unique_multiword_aliases": len(aliases),
            "intended_linked_aliases": total_linked,
            "lost_intended_links": losses}}
Path(__file__).with_suffix(".json").write_text(
    json.dumps(output, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
print(json.dumps({"cases": [{"case": r["case"], "grounded": r["checks"]["grounded"],
    "findings": r["checks"]["unsupported_claims"]} for r in results],
    "alias_tokenisation": output["alias_tokenisation"]}, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2))

+# Independent claim-check audit, round 2
+
+Reviewed HEAD **a12762b**, against the real local graph, with diff `git diff 0c441e0...HEAD`. No tracked files were edited. All new scratch files are under `audit_evidence_claims/r2/`; the pre-existing `brief.md` and `log.txt` were left alone.
+
+## BLOCKERS
+
+**Unsupported claims still return `grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`.** Every attack below was executed; these are not hypothetical parser concerns. Except the explicitly identified union-evidence case, they use `Engine().check(question, answer)`. Truth here means support in the retrieved evidence, not an external medical judgment.
+
+Question keys are exact inputs:
+
+| Key | Question |
+|---|---|
+| Q1 | `Which companies are developing osimertinib?` |
+| Q2 | `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?` |
+| Q3 | `What does osimertinib target?` |
+| Q4 | `Which drugs act on ALK?` |
+| Q5 | `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` |
+| Q6 | `What is the capital of France?` |
+| QC | `What does crizotinib target?` |
+| QF | `What does fulvestrant target?` |
+
+### B1. The original reversed noun relation is NOT FIXED; passive detection also reverses active claims
+
+| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q3 | `EGFR is an osimertinib inhibitor.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q3 | `EGFR is what inhibited osimertinib.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q2 | `Osimertinib is what evaluated NCT03909334.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q5 | `Osimertinib is what sponsored AstraZeneca.` | True; `[]` |
+
+The sole Q3 evidence is `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`, the opposite direction. Q2 says the trial evaluates the drug; Q5 says the company sponsors trials of the drug.
+
+The noun fallback at `src/claims.py:407-411` fills an absent endpoint using entity type after `_group` selects the nearest entity (`:382-383`), reconstructing the supported inverse instead of checking the written subject. The passive heuristic at `:401-404` treats any `BE` token within three words as an auxiliary. In `is what inhibited`, `inhibited` is an active past-tense verb in a cleft, but the checker reverses it.
+
+The first answer is **an exact R1 reproduction**, not a new variant. It is absent from the regression attack list at `tests/test_claims.py:81-122`, despite the repair table's statement that all five R1 direction examples were repaired. Sources: `replay_main_results.json` (`reverse_noun`), `spec_probe_results.json` (`passive_cleft`, `passive_cleft_trial`, `passive_cleft_sponsor`).
+
+### B2. A repeated preposition or an adverb hides an additional relation endpoint
+
+All with Q3:
+
+| Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|
+| `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and on ALK.` | True; `[]` |
+| `Osimertinib is an inhibitor of EGFR and of ALK.` | True; `[]` |
+| `Osimertinib inhibits EGFR and also ALK.` | True; `[]` |
+| Control: `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and ALK.` | False; `relation: OSIMERTINIB — acts — ALK` |
+
+No Q3 evidence mentions ALK. `_group` stops at `on`, `of`, or `also` (`src/claims.py:342-357`). `_clauses` does not split an elided predicate fragment (`:324-341`), and the later wording pass exempts all these grammar/relation words (`:552-553`). The extra named entity is left with no relation obligation. This also affects `target of`: with Q3+QC evidence, `EGFR is a target of osimertinib and of crizotinib.` passes although there is no retrieved crizotinib–EGFR edge. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `endpoint_preposition`, `endpoint_noun`, `endpoint_adverb`, `endpoint_control`, `noun_target_second`.
+
+### B3. Pronouns resolve before the preceding clause is processed; verbatim evidence loses trial subjects
+
+**Clause timing.** Use the unmodified union of evidence retrieved for Q3 and QC, with their linked question entities and the normal vocabulary/linker:
+
+```text
+Osimertinib acts on EGFR. Crizotinib acts on ALK and it inhibits EGFR.
+```
+
+Observed: True; `[]`. The union contains osimertinib–EGFR and crizotinib–ALK, but no crizotinib–EGFR edge. Replacing `and` with `.`, so that the last sentence is `It inhibits EGFR.`, gives False with `relation: CRIZOTINIB — inhibits — EGFR`. Omitting `it` (`... and inhibits EGFR.`) also correctly fails. The typed variant `... while this drug inhibits EGFR.` wrongly passes.
+
+`_tokenise` resolves the entire unit at `src/claims.py:228`, before `_clauses` and the antecedent updates at `:259-261`; resolution at `:302-303` therefore uses the previous unit's osimertinib. The clause reader updates state too late to change the already substituted pronoun. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `pronoun_stale`, `pronoun_stale2`, `pronoun_fresh`, `elided`.
+
+**Verbatim antecedent.** With Q2:
+
+```text
+NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB. It is phase 3.
+```
+
+Observed: True; `[]`. The named trial is PHASE2. In contrast, `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib. It is phase 3.` correctly fails. At `src/claims.py:221-226`, the exact-statement shortcut chooses the first result of `linker(bare)` as subject. The linker does not produce trial entities; NCT handling exists separately at `:274-275`. Thus the copied statement installs OSIMERTINIB as antecedent, admitting phase 3 from other trials of that drug. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `verbatim_antecedent_trial`, `paraphrase_trial_wrong_phase_control`.
+
+### B4. Numeric ownership still migrates between drugs and between attribute types
+
+| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials with carboplatin.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q5 | Control: `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials.` | False; `number: 10` |
+| Q5 | Diagnostic: `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 14 trials with carboplatin.` | False; `number: 14` |
+| Q4 | `PLB1003 has 1 clinical trial.` | True; `[]` |
+
+The retrieved AstraZeneca count is **14 for osimertinib**, **10 for carboplatin**; it does not establish ten trials combining them. `_owner` searches after a number before looking before it (`src/claims.py:460-468`, `:524`). The appended carboplatin captures the count even though the claim is about osimertinib; the incorrect 10 passes and the osimertinib count 14 fails. The 14 example is a diagnostic of assignment, not a claim that the combination is supported.
+
+PLB1003 evidence explicitly says `max stage PHASE_1; 0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph`. Numeric checking matches any number in a scoped statement (`:524-525`), so phase **1** satisfies a claimed count of **1** despite the count being **0**. This is a specific, reproducible limitation of counting, not merely a distant-entity issue. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `number_drug_other`, `count_after`, `count_vs_14`, `count_morph`.
+
+### B5. Action validation matches `agonist` inside `antagonist`
+
+Question QF; sole evidence: `FULVESTRANT acts on ESR1 (antagonist)`.
+
+- `Fulvestrant activates ESR1.` → True; `[]`.
+- `Fulvestrant is an agonist of ESR1.` → True; `[]`.
+- Control: `Fulvestrant is an antagonist of ESR1.` → True, correctly.
+
+`ACTION_WORDS` includes `agonist` (`src/claims.py:73-74`); `:454-455` uses substring containment rather than matching the action token/field. Consequently the action described in the evidence satisfies its opposite. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `action_antagonist`, `action_noun_agonist`, `action_control_antagonist`.
+
+### B6. Heading handling discards claims; the bare-item verbatim pass bypasses joined headings
+
+All with Q3 and its sole EGFR-inhibition statement:
+
+| Exact answer (`\n` denotes a newline) | Observed |
+|---|---|
+| `osimertinib is safe:` | True; `[]` |
+| `osimertinib is discontinued:\nOsimertinib acts on EGFR.` | True; `[]` |
+| `these drugs are safe:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
+| `these drugs do not act on EGFR:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
+| `EGFR acts on these drugs:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
+| Control: `these drugs are safe:\n- osimertinib` | False; `wording: safe` |
+
+`_units` defers any non-bullet line ending in a colon and never emits it if no bullet follows (`src/claims.py:170-188`). Thus ordinary content words can disappear entirely. When a bullet does follow, joining does retain the heading (`:177-183`), but the shortcut tests **either** the combined unit **or the original bare item** against evidence (`:221`). A verbatim evidence item exempts an unsafe, negated, or reversed heading. These are separate paths; fixing dangling headings alone would leave the bare-item bypass. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `discarded_heading`, `discarded_heading_before_prose`, `verbatim_under_unsafe_heading`, `verbatim_under_negated_heading`, `verbatim_under_reversed_heading`, `ordinary_bullet_heading_control`.
+
+### B7. The META exception accepts biomedical claims, including an invented phase with zero evidence
+
+| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q6 | `This trial is phase 4.` | True; `[]`; **evidence_count=0** |
+| Q2 | `This trial is phase 3.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q2 | `This drug is not in trials.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q1 | `It has 14 trials.` | True; `[]` |
+
+These are not graph refusals. An unresolved pronoun should be reported, as the docstring explicitly says (`src/claims.py:22-24`). The early pass at `:233-236` permits every `FUNCTION`/negation word and examines only word tokens, ignoring numbers. `phase` and `trials` are grammar words. The global phase check also skips any one-digit phase whenever a linker is present (`src/query.py:504-508`), even if evidence is empty. A missing-subject phase fragment takes a similar early exit (`claims.py:247-254`).
+
+Control: Q3 / `It inhibits EGFR.` correctly produces `no_antecedent: it`; the bug is the overbroad refusal exception, not an intentional general policy to accept unresolved claims. A legitimate `This graph cannot answer that question.` passes. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `meta_france_unsupported_phase`, `meta_missing_typed_phase`, `meta_missing_negation`, `meta_missing_count`, `no_antecedent_control`.
+
+### B8. Refused product spans become opaque entities, swallowing qualifiers and negation
+
+All with Q3; all return True; `[]`:
+
+- `osimertinib is liposomal.`
+- `osimertinib is a biosimilar.`
+- `Osimertinib is not liposomal.`
+- `liposomal Tagrisso.`
+
+The retrieved statement establishes only the EGFR action, not these product claims. `src/query.py:128-141` deliberately emits `Refused` spans and includes neighboring words. `src/claims.py:278-288` accepts them as entity placeholders; `:231-240` rejects `Missing` but does not reject `Refused`. The consumed span, including `not`, is no longer checked as wording or negation. In the Tagrisso example, the new alias exception (`query.py:495-499`, `:543-544`) admits the isolated brand token even though its enclosing product was refused. Sources: `standards_probe.json`, cases `refused_liposomal`, `refused_biosimilar`, `refused_negation`, `refused_alias`.
+
+### B9. Citation stripping still accepts arbitrary content after `ORG:`
+
+Q3 / `Osimertinib [ORG:is safe] acts on EGFR.` → True; `[]`.
+
+Q3 / `Osimertinib [ORG:inhibits ALK and] acts on EGFR.` → True; `[]`.
+
+The regex `ORG:[^\]]+` (`src/claims.py:136-137`) treats arbitrary bracket text as a citation, and `_units` deletes it at `:167`. It neither validates a source identifier nor checks membership in retrieved records. The ordinary R1 bracket example is now caught, but adding the citation prefix restores a text-deletion bypass. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `citation_embedded_claim`, `citation_embedded_wrong_relation`.
+
+## SHOULD FIX
+

+|---|---:|---:|
+| Q1 | 2/6 | 0/6 |

+"""Round 2 independent attacks; reads local graph, writes only adjacent JSON."""

+    ('discarded_heading', QT, 'osimertinib is safe:'),
+    ('discarded_heading_before_prose', QT, 'osimertinib is discontinued:\nOsimertinib acts on EGFR.'),
+    ('verbatim_under_unsafe_heading', QT, 'these drugs are safe:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)'),
+    ('verbatim_under_negated_heading', QT, 'these drugs do not act on EGFR:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)'),

+    ('meta_missing_phase', QR, 'It is phase 3.'),
+    ('meta_missing_typed_phase', QR, 'This trial is phase 3.'),
+    ('meta_missing_negation', QR, 'This drug is not in trials.'),
+    ('meta_missing_count', QS, 'It has 14 trials.'),

+    ('meta_no_subject_negation', QR, 'No trials.'),
+    ('meta_context_phase', QR, 'NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib.\nThis trial is phase 3.'),
+    ('verbatim_antecedent_trial', QR, 'NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB. It is phase 3.'),
+    ('verbatim_antecedent_typed_trial', QR, 'NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB. This trial is phase 3.'),
+    ('verbatim_phase_changed_punctuation', QR, 'NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE NOT RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB.'),
+    ('citation_embedded_claim', QT, 'Osimertinib [ORG:is safe] acts on EGFR.'),
+    ('citation_embedded_negation', QT, 'Osimertinib [ORG:does not] act on EGFR.'),
+    ('citation_embedded_wrong_relation', QT, 'Osimertinib [ORG:inhibits ALK and] acts on EGFR.'),

+    ('partial_phase_1_from_12', QA, 'CONTELTINIB is phase 1.'),
+    ('question_not_claim', QT, 'Does osimertinib act on EGFR?'),

+# Independent claim-check audit, round 2
+
+Reviewed HEAD **a12762b**, against the real local graph, with diff `git diff 0c441e0...HEAD`. No tracked files were edited. All new scratch files are under `audit_evidence_claims/r2/`; the pre-existing `brief.md` and `log.txt` were left alone.
+
+## BLOCKERS
+
+**Unsupported claims still return `grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`.** Every attack below was executed; these are not hypothetical parser concerns. Except the explicitly identified union-evidence case, they use `Engine().check(question, answer)`. Truth here means support in the retrieved evidence, not an external medical judgment.
+
+Question keys are exact inputs:
+
+| Key | Question |
+|---|---|
+| Q1 | `Which companies are developing osimertinib?` |
+| Q2 | `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?` |
+| Q3 | `What does osimertinib target?` |
+| Q4 | `Which drugs act on ALK?` |
+| Q5 | `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` |
+| Q6 | `What is the capital of France?` |
+| QC | `What does crizotinib target?` |
+| QF | `What does fulvestrant target?` |
+
+### B1. The original reversed noun relation is NOT FIXED; passive detection also reverses active claims
+
+| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q3 | `EGFR is an osimertinib inhibitor.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q3 | `EGFR is what inhibited osimertinib.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q2 | `Osimertinib is what evaluated NCT03909334.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q5 | `Osimertinib is what sponsored AstraZeneca.` | True; `[]` |
+
+The sole Q3 evidence is `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`, the opposite direction. Q2 says the trial evaluates the drug; Q5 says the company sponsors trials of the drug.
+
+The noun fallback at `src/claims.py:407-411` fills an absent endpoint using entity type after `_group` selects the nearest entity (`:382-383`), reconstructing the supported inverse instead of checking the written subject. The passive heuristic at `:401-404` treats any `BE` token within three words as an auxiliary. In `is what inhibited`, `inhibited` is an active past-tense verb in a cleft, but the checker reverses it.
+
+The first answer is **an exact R1 reproduction**, not a new variant. It is absent from the regression attack list at `tests/test_claims.py:81-122`, despite the repair table's statement that all five R1 direction examples were repaired. Sources: `replay_main_results.json` (`reverse_noun`), `spec_probe_results.json` (`passive_cleft`, `passive_cleft_trial`, `passive_cleft_sponsor`).
+
+### B2. A repeated preposition or an adverb hides an additional relation endpoint
+
+All with Q3:
+
+| Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|
+| `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and on ALK.` | True; `[]` |
+| `Osimertinib is an inhibitor of EGFR and of ALK.` | True; `[]` |
+| `Osimertinib inhibits EGFR and also ALK.` | True; `[]` |
+| Control: `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and ALK.` | False; `relation: OSIMERTINIB — acts — ALK` |
+
+No Q3 evidence mentions ALK. `_group` stops at `on`, `of`, or `also` (`src/claims.py:342-357`). `_clauses` does not split an elided predicate fragment (`:324-341`), and the later wording pass exempts all these grammar/relation words (`:552-553`). The extra named entity is left with no relation obligation. This also affects `target of`: with Q3+QC evidence, `EGFR is a target of osimertinib and of crizotinib.` passes although there is no retrieved crizotinib–EGFR edge. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `endpoint_preposition`, `endpoint_noun`, `endpoint_adverb`, `endpoint_control`, `noun_target_second`.
+
+### B3. Pronouns resolve before the preceding clause is processed; verbatim evidence loses trial subjects
+
+**Clause timing.** Use the unmodified union of evidence retrieved for Q3 and QC, with their linked question entities and the normal vocabulary/linker:
+
+```text
+Osimertinib acts on EGFR. Crizotinib acts on ALK and it inhibits EGFR.
+```
+
+Observed: True; `[]`. The union contains osimertinib–EGFR and crizotinib–ALK, but no crizotinib–EGFR edge. Replacing `and` with `.`, so that the last sentence is `It inhibits EGFR.`, gives False with `relation: CRIZOTINIB — inhibits — EGFR`. Omitting `it` (`... and inhibits EGFR.`) also correctly fails. The typed variant `... while this drug inhibits EGFR.` wrongly passes.
+
+`_tokenise` resolves the entire unit at `src/claims.py:228`, before `_clauses` and the antecedent updates at `:259-261`; resolution at `:302-303` therefore uses the previous unit's osimertinib. The clause reader updates state too late to change the already substituted pronoun. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `pronoun_stale`, `pronoun_stale2`, `pronoun_fresh`, `elided`.
+
+**Verbatim antecedent.** With Q2:
+
+```text
+NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB. It is phase 3.
+```
+
+Observed: True; `[]`. The named trial is PHASE2. In contrast, `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib. It is phase 3.` correctly fails. At `src/claims.py:221-226`, the exact-statement shortcut chooses the first result of `linker(bare)` as subject. The linker does not produce trial entities; NCT handling exists separately at `:274-275`. Thus the copied statement installs OSIMERTINIB as antecedent, admitting phase 3 from other trials of that drug. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `verbatim_antecedent_trial`, `paraphrase_trial_wrong_phase_control`.
+
+### B4. Numeric ownership still migrates between drugs and between attribute types
+
+| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials with carboplatin.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q5 | Control: `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials.` | False; `number: 10` |
+| Q5 | Diagnostic: `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 14 trials with carboplatin.` | False; `number: 14` |
+| Q4 | `PLB1003 has 1 clinical trial.` | True; `[]` |
+
+The retrieved AstraZeneca count is **14 for osimertinib**, **10 for carboplatin**; it does not establish ten trials combining them. `_owner` searches after a number before looking before it (`src/claims.py:460-468`, `:524`). The appended carboplatin captures the count even though the claim is about osimertinib; the incorrect 10 passes and the osimertinib count 14 fails. The 14 example is a diagnostic of assignment, not a claim that the combination is supported.
+
+PLB1003 evidence explicitly says `max stage PHASE_1; 0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph`. Numeric checking matches any number in a scoped statement (`:524-525`), so phase **1** satisfies a claimed count of **1** despite the count being **0**. This is a specific, reproducible limitation of counting, not merely a distant-entity issue. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `number_drug_other`, `count_after`, `count_vs_14`, `count_morph`.
+
+### B5. Action validation matches `agonist` inside `antagonist`
+
+Question QF; sole evidence: `FULVESTRANT acts on ESR1 (antagonist)`.
+
+- `Fulvestrant activates ESR1.` → True; `[]`.
+- `Fulvestrant is an agonist of ESR1.` → True; `[]`.
+- Control: `Fulvestrant is an antagonist of ESR1.` → True, correctly.
+
+`ACTION_WORDS` includes `agonist` (`src/claims.py:73-74`); `:454-455` uses substring containment rather than matching the action token/field. Consequently the action described in the evidence satisfies its opposite. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `action_antagonist`, `action_noun_agonist`, `action_control_antagonist`.
+
+### B6. Heading handling discards claims; the bare-item verbatim pass bypasses joined headings
+
+All with Q3 and its sole EGFR-inhibition statement:
+
+| Exact answer (`\n` denotes a newline) | Observed |
+|---|---|
+| `osimertinib is safe:` | True; `[]` |
+| `osimertinib is discontinued:\nOsimertinib acts on EGFR.` | True; `[]` |
+| `these drugs are safe:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
+| `these drugs do not act on EGFR:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
+| `EGFR acts on these drugs:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
+| Control: `these drugs are safe:\n- osimertinib` | False; `wording: safe` |
+
+`_units` defers any non-bullet line ending in a colon and never emits it if no bullet follows (`src/claims.py:170-188`). Thus ordinary content words can disappear entirely. When a bullet does follow, joining does retain the heading (`:177-183`), but the shortcut tests **either** the combined unit **or the original bare item** against evidence (`:221`). A verbatim evidence item exempts an unsafe, negated, or reversed heading. These are separate paths; fixing dangling headings alone would leave the bare-item bypass. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `discarded_heading`, `discarded_heading_before_prose`, `verbatim_under_unsafe_heading`, `verbatim_under_negated_heading`, `verbatim_under_reversed_heading`, `ordinary_bullet_heading_control`.
+
+### B7. The META exception accepts biomedical claims, including an invented phase with zero evidence
+
+| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q6 | `This trial is phase 4.` | True; `[]`; **evidence_count=0** |
+| Q2 | `This trial is phase 3.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q2 | `This drug is not in trials.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q1 | `It has 14 trials.` | True; `[]` |
+
+These are not graph refusals. An unresolved pronoun should be reported, as the docstring explicitly says (`src/claims.py:22-24`). The early pass at `:233-236` permits every `FUNCTION`/negation word and examines only word tokens, ignoring numbers. `phase` and `trials` are grammar words. The global phase check also skips any one-digit phase whenever a linker is present (`src/query.py:504-508`), even if evidence is empty. A missing-subject phase fragment takes a similar early exit (`claims.py:247-254`).
+
+Control: Q3 / `It inhibits EGFR.` correctly produces `no_antecedent: it`; the bug is the overbroad refusal exception, not an intentional general policy to accept unresolved claims. A legitimate `This graph cannot answer that question.` passes. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `meta_france_unsupported_phase`, `meta_missing_typed_phase`, `meta_missing_negation`, `meta_missing_count`, `no_antecedent_control`.
+
+### B8. Refused product spans become opaque entities, swallowing qualifiers and negation
+
+All with Q3; all return True; `[]`:
+
+- `osimertinib is liposomal.`
+- `osimertinib is a biosimilar.`
+- `Osimertinib is not liposomal.`
+- `liposomal Tagrisso.`
+
+The retrieved statement establishes only the EGFR action, not these product claims. `src/query.py:128-141` deliberately emits `Refused` spans and includes neighboring words. `src/claims.py:278-288` accepts them as entity placeholders; `:231-240` rejects `Missing` but does not reject `Refused`. The consumed span, including `not`, is no longer checked as wording or negation. In the Tagrisso example, the new alias exception (`query.py:495-499`, `:543-544`) admits the isolated brand token even though its enclosing product was refused. Sources: `standards_probe.json`, cases `refused_liposomal`, `refused_biosimilar`, `refused_negation`, `refused_alias`.
+
+### B9. Citation stripping still accepts arbitrary content after `ORG:`
+
+Q3 / `Osimertinib [ORG:is safe] acts on EGFR.` → True; `[]`.
+
+Q3 / `Osimertinib [ORG:inhibits ALK and] acts on EGFR.` → True; `[]`.
+
+The regex `ORG:[^\]]+` (`src/claims.py:136-137`) treats arbitrary bracket text as a citation, and `_units` deletes it at `:167`. It neither validates a source identifier nor checks membership in retrieved records. The ordinary R1 bracket example is now caught, but adding the citation prefix restores a text-deletion bypass. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `citation_embedded_claim`, `citation_embedded_wrong_relation`.
+
+## SHOULD FIX
+
+### S1. False positives are much lower on the original corpus, but ordinary faithful forms still fail
+
+The exact R1 corpus was rerun without rewriting its answers:
+
+| Demo question | R1 faithful answers flagged | R2 faithful answers flagged |
+|---|---:|---:|
+| Q1 | 2/6 | 0/6 |
+| Q2 | 2/6 | 0/6 |
+| Q3 | 1/6 | 0/6 |
+| Q4 | 3/6 | 0/6 |
+| Q5 | 2/6 | 1/6 |
+| Q6, refusals separately | 6/6 | 2/6 |
+
+Evidence-answer false positives improved from **10/30 (33.3%) to 1/30 (3.3%)**. This small hand-authored diagnostic corpus does not estimate a production LLM rate or support saying that most faithful answers fail. The remaining evidence-answer rejection is Q5 / `AstraZeneca supports trials studying osimertinib in NSCLC.` → False, `wording: supports`; the repair notes explicitly identify this as intentional strictness.
+
+Other confirmed faithful/refusal failures:
+
+| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q3 | `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and it inhibits EGFR.` | False; `no_antecedent: it` |
+| Q3 | `Osimertinib (Tagrisso) acts on EGFR.` | False; `wording: tagrisso` |
+| Q3 | `AZD 9291 acts on EGFR.` | False; `number: 9291` |
+| Q6 | `The graph covers NSCLC drugs and trials, so it cannot answer this question.` | False; `no_antecedent: it` |
+| Q6 | `I cannot determine the capital of France from the retrieved evidence.` | False; `entity: France`, `no_subject: i cannot determine capital france` |
+
+The first is the same within-unit antecedent timing error as B3. Repeated aliases are lost because the linker deduplicates by drug ID (`src/query.py:163-168`) before the tokenizer replaces all surface forms (`claims.py:281-288`). The spaced development code links correctly, but the global numeric pass does not exempt digits inside entity spans (`query.py:506-511`). Controls `Tagrisso acts on EGFR.` and `AZD9291 acts on EGFR.` pass. Evidence is in `replay_faithful_results.json`, `standards_probe.json`, and `spec_probe_results.json`.
+
+### S2. Documentation and the repair table still promise more than the code establishes
+
+- `README.md:508-520` and `README.ko.md:354-360` make categorical direction/action/ownership claims and say negations are always reported. B1–B9 contradict these claims. The acknowledged residual case—rearranging one evidence statement's words—does not explain discarded headings, source-like text, new words in refused spans, or stale antecedents. Even intended verbatim and META exceptions contradict “always”.
+- `src/claims.py:10-24` promises checked relations and reported unresolved pronouns; B1, B2, B3 and B7 refute those statements. `:15-17` says attribute evidence names every clause entity, whereas numeric `scoped()` (`:473-477`) uses only the first clause entity and heuristic owner.
+- `src/query.py:447` says a generated answer is verified to say nothing absent from evidence; `:461-466` retains categorical sentence-level guarantees. It was not updated to explain the clause parser's failure modes.
+- `web/template.html:263-265` and `web/try.html:69-71` retain categorical guarantees about unsupported relations/clinical claims. The softer success text at `web/try.html:142` is an improvement, but still says checked relations and values match when B1/B4/B5 demonstrate the contrary.
+- `CODEX_CLAIMS_R1_VERDICT.md:9` says the five R1 direction examples were repaired. The exact reversed noun still passes and is missing from `tests/test_claims.py:81-122`. Preserve the actual R1 cases, rather than treating the 33-case regression list as exhaustive.
+
+The independent standards review found no separate coding-standard document to enforce and no hard style violation. Its behavioral/documentation findings are recorded separately in [standards_findings.md](standards_findings.md); the independent spec analysis is in [spec_findings.md](spec_findings.md). Those axes do not substitute for each other.

+
+### Regressions, templates, linker and scope
+
+- **Tests:** `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` → **128 passed**. The brief's 79 is the older suite size. The initial run had a cp949 subprocess-reader warning; the UTF-8 rerun passed all 128 in 6.24s without warnings (`replay_pytest_utf8.txt`). TEMP/TMP stayed under `r2/replay_temp`, with bytecode and pytest cache disabled.
+- **Templates:** all **5/5 evidence template bodies** pass generated-prose checking after the same `Scope:` removal as the normal answer path. Full Scope-inclusive templates still fail. The France refusal template fails generated checking (`no_antecedent: it`, `no_subject: no entity question matched`); ordinary `ask()` uses its separate refusal path. All exact template texts/outputs are in `replay_faithful_results.json`.
+- **Original linker experiment:** **7,610 dictionary keys / 1,066 drugs / 2,634 unique multiword aliases**, zero lost intended links from the abbreviation gate. `is a` stays unlinked; `AZD 9291`, `osimertinib mesylate`, `trastuzumab deruxtecan`, and `pemetrexed disodium` retain intended links. This replays the original gate-on/gate-off experiment, not an old-commit execution.
+- **New punctuation handling:** there is no `link_entities` implementation change between 0c441e0 and a12762b; the new sanitization is in `claims.py:277`. `Osimertinib acts on EGFR!` passes. The question `Which drugs act on ALK!` (likewise `:` or `;`) still links no entity and yields `intent=unsupported`, while `?` works (`query.py:120`). This is an unaddressed question-path limitation, not a new linker regression.
+- **Dictionary-to-parser coverage:** among 2,634 stored multiword aliases, 2,566 link an intended drug ID; five of these lose that ID during claim tokenization (two parenthesized chemical aliases and three parent/salt overlap cases). Exact strings and entity traces are in `standards_probe.json`. These counts describe parser discrepancies, not five newly introduced linker regressions.
+- **Successful controls:** explicit reversed active relations, all original wrong named pairs, old clinical/negation attacks, Korean/mixed text, correct basic `target of`, correct `EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib`, and simple elided-subject `and inhibits` cases were exercised. Their passes/failures are retained alongside attacks. Fixes demonstrably improved behavior; the blockers remain independently reproducible.
+

New-design probes are `root_design_probe.py`, `spec_probe.py`, and `standards_probe.py`; their adjacent JSON files contain exact input, output and retrieved evidence. Final tracked/staged diffs were empty; `git status --short` listed only the pre-existing untracked `audit_evidence_claims/r2/` tree.

+    ('meta_missing_typed_phase', QR, 'This trial is phase 3.'),
+    ('meta_missing_negation', QR, 'This drug is not in trials.'),
+    ('meta_missing_count', QS, 'It has 14 trials.'),
+    ('meta_no_subject_phase', QR, 'phase 3.'),

+    ('verbatim_antecedent_trial', QR, 'NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB. It is phase 3.'),
+    ('verbatim_antecedent_typed_trial', QR, 'NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB. This trial is phase 3.'),
+    ('verbatim_phase_changed_punctuation', QR, 'NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE NOT RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB.'),
+    ('citation_embedded_claim', QT, 'Osimertinib [ORG:is safe] acts on EGFR.'),
+    ('citation_embedded_negation', QT, 'Osimertinib [ORG:does not] act on EGFR.'),
+    ('citation_embedded_wrong_relation', QT, 'Osimertinib [ORG:inhibits ALK and] acts on EGFR.'),
+    ('clinical_removed_scope', QT, 'Osimertinib is for lung cancer.'),

+for cid, q, answer in cases:

+    entities = engine.kg.link_entities(q)

+Path(__file__).with_suffix('.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')

# Independent spec-axis attacks on a12762b

Reproduce from repository root with `python -B audit_evidence_claims/r2/spec_probe.py`. All 57 exact inputs, full checks, complete retrieved statements, and parser units are in `spec_probe_results.json`. Only scratch files under `audit_evidence_claims/r2/` were written. No source files were changed.

## BLOCKERS

All unsupported examples below returned `grounded=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]` using the real graph. These are failures of stated relations, entity binding, or numeric attributes, not externally asserted clinical facts.

### S1. Repeated prepositions silently remove a second relation endpoint

- Question: `What does osimertinib target?`
- Answer: `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and on ALK.`
- Other passing answers: `Osimertinib is an inhibitor of EGFR and of ALK.` and `Osimertinib inhibits EGFR and also ALK.`
- Retrieved evidence: `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`, with no osimertinib--ALK relation.
- Control: `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and ALK.` returns false with `relation: OSIMERTINIB — acts — ALK`.
- Cause: `src/claims.py:328-338` does not split without a second predicate; `_group` at `src/claims.py:343-358` ends at `on`, `of`, or `also`. Relation validation at `src/claims.py:382-383` and `447-453` only visits that truncated group; attributes exempt remaining grammar at `src/claims.py:552-553`. No check verifies every linked endpoint is accounted for.
- Also reproduced nominal `target of`: union unmodified target evidence for osimertinib and crizotinib; `EGFR is a target of osimertinib and of crizotinib.` passes despite no crizotinib--EGFR edge. The new noun-form support inherits this bypass.
- Spec requirement: "A stated relation ... must hold between the same entities"; Round 2 explicitly requests clause splitting and noun-form attacks.

### S2. Copular clefts are mistaken for passive voice, reversing false relations

| Exact question | Exact passing answer |
|---|---|
| `What does osimertinib target?` | `EGFR is what inhibited osimertinib.` |
| `What does osimertinib target?` | `EGFR is what targeted osimertinib.` |
| `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?` | `Osimertinib is what evaluated NCT03909334.` |
| `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` | `Osimertinib is what sponsored AstraZeneca.` |

Evidence supports the opposite directions: osimertinib acts on EGFR, NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib, AstraZeneca sponsors trials evaluating osimertinib. `is what inhibited/evaluated/sponsored` contains an active relative clause, not a passive construction.

Cause: `src/claims.py:401-404` flips endpoints whenever a form of BE appears in the preceding three tokens. The presence of `is` in the separate copular clause suffices. Control `Osimertinib is inhibited by EGFR.` is correctly rejected for direction. Requirement: stated direction and explicit Round-2 passive detection attack.

### S3. Within-sentence pronouns bind to the preceding sentence rather than preceding clause

- Retrieve the union of unmodified evidence for `What does osimertinib target?` and `What does crizotinib target?`.
- Answer: `Osimertinib acts on EGFR. Crizotinib acts on ALK and it inhibits EGFR.`
- Also passes with `while this drug inhibits EGFR.` in place of `and it inhibits EGFR.`.
- Evidence supports osimertinib--EGFR and crizotinib--ALK, but not crizotinib--EGFR. The second sentence makes the latter claim.
- Control: `Osimertinib acts on EGFR. Crizotinib acts on ALK. It inhibits EGFR.` is correctly rejected with `relation: CRIZOTINIB — inhibits — EGFR`.
- Cause: `src/claims.py:228` tokenizes and resolves all pronouns before iterating over clauses; `src/claims.py:299-300` uses the old antecedent, while state is updated only at `src/claims.py:259-261`. Thus the `it` in the second sentence is frozen to osimertinib.
- Requirement: claimed behavior in `src/claims.py:22-23`, "subject of the clause before".

### S4. A phase digit is accepted as a trial count

- Question: `Which drugs act on ALK?`
- Answer: `PLB1003 has 1 clinical trial.`
- Also passes: `PLB1003: 1 trial.`
- Exact evidence: `PLB1003 acts on ALK (inhibitor; max stage PHASE_1; 0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph)`.
- Actual supported count is **0**, whereas **1** is the maximum-stage digit.
- Cause: `src/claims.py:522-527` checks any matching number in entity-scoped text, without its attribute. `_flat` converts `PHASE_1` to `phase 1`; the resulting 1 satisfies the count check.
- Requirement: "N trials must be stated for that sentence's own entities"; entity ownership alone is insufficient if the number belongs to another attribute.

### S5. Nearest-following entity still transfers a count across drugs

- Question: `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?`
- Answer: `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials with carboplatin.`
- Evidence: `Astrazeneca sponsors 14 NSCLC trial(s) evaluating OSIMERTINIB`; `Astrazeneca sponsors 10 NSCLC trial(s) evaluating CARBOPLATIN`. Neither states the asserted joint trials, and 10 belongs to carboplatin's separate count.
- Controls: remove `with carboplatin` and the incorrect 10 is rejected; replace 10 with 14 while keeping `with carboplatin` and 14 is rejected.
- Cause: `src/claims.py:460-468` searches the preferred direction before considering distance or grammar; `src/claims.py:524` prefers the next entity. `src/claims.py:473-477` then checks the number against AstraZeneca+carboplatin, dropping osimertinib from numeric scope. Although full-clause wording support is empty, every remaining word is exempt grammar/relation text.
- Requirement: counts cannot migrate between entities; explicit Round-2 nearest-entity attack.

### S6. Antagonist evidence satisfies agonist/activation claims by substring

- Question: `What does fulvestrant target?`
- Answers: `Fulvestrant activates ESR1.` and `Fulvestrant is an agonist of ESR1.`
- Exact evidence: `FULVESTRANT acts on ESR1 (antagonist)`.
- Control: `Fulvestrant is an antagonist of ESR1.` also passes, correctly.
- Cause: `src/claims.py:73-74` includes `agonist` as an activation marker, and `src/claims.py:454-455` uses unrestricted substring containment. `agonist in antagonist` is true. The action type therefore verifies its opposite.
- Requirement: action type must match (repair table R1 #7; `src/claims.py:14`). This is distinct from plain generic ACTS_ON scope.

## SHOULD FIX

1. **Faithful inline pronouns fail.** Q3, `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and it inhibits EGFR.` returns false with `no_antecedent: it`. There is an unambiguous antecedent in the preceding clause; cause is S3. Using union target evidence, `Osimertinib acts on EGFR. Crizotinib acts on ALK and it inhibits ALK.` is also falsely rejected for `OSIMERTINIB — inhibits — ALK`.
2. **Faithful possessive noun relation fails.** Q3, `Osimertinib is EGFR's inhibitor.` returns false with `wording: egfr's` and `relation_incomplete: inhibitor`. This is secondary to the blocking noun endpoint omission.

## NOTES

- Elided predicates are unchecked as well: with union target evidence, `Crizotinib acts on ALK; on EGFR.` and `Osimertinib acts on EGFR; crizotinib on EGFR.` both pass. This extends S1; omitted-subject relation with an explicit verb (`... Crizotinib acts on ALK and inhibits EGFR.`) correctly fails.
- Simple reversed `target of`, `inhibitor of`, and passive `is inhibited by` attacks are caught. `The inhibition of EGFR by osimertinib.` passes; reversed `The inhibition of osimertinib by EGFR.` is rejected.
- Independent R1 replay, complete regression tests, documentation, heading/verbatim/META and linker/alias coverage are assigned to other reviewers. No claim here estimates an overall false-negative rate from this exploratory 57-case corpus.

+# Independent claim-check audit, round 2
+
+Reviewed HEAD **a12762b**, against the real local graph, with diff `git diff 0c441e0...HEAD`. No tracked files were edited. All new scratch files are under `audit_evidence_claims/r2/`; the pre-existing `brief.md` and `log.txt` were left alone.
+
+## BLOCKERS
+
+**Unsupported claims still return `grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`.** Every attack below was executed; these are not hypothetical parser concerns. Except the explicitly identified union-evidence cases, they use `Engine().check(question, answer)` or the equivalent direct `check_groundedness` call with that question's unmodified retrieved evidence. Truth here means support in the retrieved evidence, not an external medical judgment.
+
+Question keys are exact inputs:
+
+| Key | Question |
+|---|---|
+| Q1 | `Which companies are developing osimertinib?` |
+| Q2 | `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?` |
+| Q3 | `What does osimertinib target?` |
+| Q4 | `Which drugs act on ALK?` |
+| Q5 | `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` |
+| Q6 | `What is the capital of France?` |
+| QC | `What does crizotinib target?` |
+| QF | `What does fulvestrant target?` |
+
+### B1. The original reversed noun relation is NOT FIXED; passive detection also reverses active claims
+
+| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q3 | `EGFR is an osimertinib inhibitor.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q3 | `EGFR is what inhibited osimertinib.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q2 | `Osimertinib is what evaluated NCT03909334.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q5 | `Osimertinib is what sponsored AstraZeneca.` | True; `[]` |
+
+The sole Q3 evidence is `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`, the opposite direction. Q2 says the trial evaluates the drug; Q5 says the company sponsors trials of the drug.
+
+The noun fallback at `src/claims.py:407-411` fills an absent endpoint using entity type after `_group` selects the nearest entity (`:382-383`), reconstructing the supported inverse instead of checking the written subject. The passive heuristic at `:401-404` treats any `BE` token within three words as an auxiliary. In `is what inhibited`, `inhibited` is an active past-tense verb in a cleft, but the checker reverses it.
+
+The first answer is **an exact R1 reproduction**, not a new variant. It is absent from the regression attack list at `tests/test_claims.py:81-122`, despite the repair table's statement that all five R1 direction examples were repaired. Sources: `replay_main_results.json` (`reverse_noun`), `spec_probe_results.json` (`passive_cleft`, `passive_cleft_trial`, `passive_cleft_sponsor`).
+
+### B2. A repeated preposition or an adverb hides an additional relation endpoint
+
+All with Q3:
+
+| Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|
+| `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and on ALK.` | True; `[]` |
+| `Osimertinib is an inhibitor of EGFR and of ALK.` | True; `[]` |
+| `Osimertinib inhibits EGFR and also ALK.` | True; `[]` |
+| Control: `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and ALK.` | False; `relation: OSIMERTINIB — acts — ALK` |
+
+No Q3 evidence mentions ALK. `_group` stops at `on`, `of`, or `also` (`src/claims.py:342-357`). `_clauses` does not split an elided predicate fragment (`:324-341`), and the later wording pass exempts all these grammar/relation words (`:552-553`). The extra named entity is left with no relation obligation. This also affects `target of`: with Q3+QC evidence, `EGFR is a target of osimertinib and of crizotinib.` passes although there is no retrieved crizotinib–EGFR edge. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `endpoint_preposition`, `endpoint_noun`, `endpoint_adverb`, `endpoint_control`, `noun_target_second`.
+
+### B3. Pronouns resolve before the preceding clause is processed; verbatim evidence loses trial subjects
+
+**Clause timing.** Use the unmodified union of evidence retrieved for Q3 and QC, with their linked question entities and the normal vocabulary/linker:
+
+```text
+Osimertinib acts on EGFR. Crizotinib acts on ALK and it inhibits EGFR.
+```
+
+Observed: True; `[]`. The union contains osimertinib–EGFR and crizotinib–ALK, but no crizotinib–EGFR edge. Replacing `and` with `.`, so that the last sentence is `It inhibits EGFR.`, gives False with `relation: CRIZOTINIB — inhibits — EGFR`. Omitting `it` (`... and inhibits EGFR.`) also correctly fails. The typed variant `... while this drug inhibits EGFR.` wrongly passes.
+
+`_tokenise` resolves the entire unit at `src/claims.py:228`, before `_clauses` and the antecedent updates at `:259-261`; resolution at `:299-300` therefore uses the previous unit's osimertinib. The clause reader updates state too late to change the already substituted pronoun. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `pronoun_stale`, `pronoun_stale2`, `pronoun_fresh`, `elided`.
+
+**Verbatim antecedent.** With Q2:
+
+```text
+NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB. It is phase 3.
+```
+
+Observed: True; `[]`. The named trial is PHASE2. In contrast, `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib. It is phase 3.` correctly fails. At `src/claims.py:221-226`, the exact-statement shortcut chooses the first result of `linker(bare)` as subject. The linker does not produce trial entities; NCT handling exists separately at `:274-275`. Thus the copied statement installs OSIMERTINIB as antecedent, admitting phase 3 from other trials of that drug. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `verbatim_antecedent_trial`, `paraphrase_trial_wrong_phase_control`.
+
+### B4. Numeric ownership still migrates between drugs and between attribute types
+
+| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials with carboplatin.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q5 | Control: `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials.` | False; `number: 10` |
+| Q5 | Diagnostic: `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 14 trials with carboplatin.` | False; `number: 14` |
+| Q4 | `PLB1003 has 1 clinical trial.` | True; `[]` |
+
+The retrieved AstraZeneca count is **14 for osimertinib**, **10 for carboplatin**; it does not establish ten trials combining them. `_owner` searches after a number before looking before it (`src/claims.py:460-468`, `:524`). The appended carboplatin captures the count even though the claim is about osimertinib; the incorrect 10 passes and the osimertinib count 14 fails. The 14 example is a diagnostic of assignment, not a claim that the combination is supported.
+
+PLB1003 evidence explicitly says `max stage PHASE_1; 0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph`. Numeric checking matches any number in a scoped statement (`:524-525`), so phase **1** satisfies a claimed count of **1** despite the count being **0**. This is a specific, reproducible limitation of counting, not merely a distant-entity issue. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `number_drug_other`, `count_after`, `count_vs_14`, `count_morph`.
+
+### B5. Action validation matches `agonist` inside `antagonist`
+
+Question QF; sole evidence: `FULVESTRANT acts on ESR1 (antagonist)`.
+
+- `Fulvestrant activates ESR1.` → True; `[]`.
+- `Fulvestrant is an agonist of ESR1.` → True; `[]`.
+- Control: `Fulvestrant is an antagonist of ESR1.` → True, correctly.
+
+`ACTION_WORDS` includes `agonist` (`src/claims.py:73-74`); `:454-455` uses substring containment rather than matching the action token/field. Consequently the action described in the evidence satisfies its opposite. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `action_antagonist`, `action_noun_agonist`, `action_control_antagonist`.
+
+### B6. Heading handling discards claims; the bare-item verbatim pass bypasses joined headings
+
+All with Q3 and its sole EGFR-inhibition statement:
+
+| Exact answer (`\n` denotes a newline) | Observed |
+|---|---|
+| `osimertinib is safe:` | True; `[]` |
+| `osimertinib is discontinued:\nOsimertinib acts on EGFR.` | True; `[]` |
+| `these drugs are safe:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
+| `these drugs do not act on EGFR:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
+| `EGFR acts on these drugs:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
+| Control: `these drugs are safe:\n- osimertinib` | False; `wording: safe` |
+
+`_units` defers any non-bullet line ending in a colon and never emits it if no bullet follows (`src/claims.py:170-188`). Thus ordinary content words can disappear entirely. When a bullet does follow, joining does retain the heading (`:177-183`), but the shortcut tests **either** the combined unit **or the original bare item** against evidence (`:221`). A verbatim evidence item exempts an unsafe, negated, or reversed heading. These are separate paths; fixing dangling headings alone would leave the bare-item bypass. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `discarded_heading`, `discarded_heading_before_prose`, `verbatim_under_unsafe_heading`, `verbatim_under_negated_heading`, `verbatim_under_reversed_heading`, `ordinary_bullet_heading_control`.
+
+### B7. The META exception accepts biomedical claims, including an invented phase with zero evidence
+
+| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q6 | `This trial is phase 4.` | True; `[]`; **evidence_count=0** |
+| Q2 | `This trial is phase 3.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q2 | `This drug is not in trials.` | True; `[]` |
+| Q1 | `It has 14 trials.` | True; `[]` |
+
+These are not graph refusals. An unresolved pronoun should be reported, as the docstring explicitly says (`src/claims.py:22-24`). The early pass at `:233-236` permits every `FUNCTION`/negation word and examines only word tokens, ignoring numbers. `phase` and `trials` are grammar words. The global phase check also skips any one-digit phase whenever a linker is present (`src/query.py:504-508`), even if evidence is empty. A missing-subject phase fragment takes a similar early exit (`claims.py:247-254`).
+
+Control: Q3 / `It inhibits EGFR.` correctly produces `no_antecedent: it`; the bug is the overbroad refusal exception, not an intentional general policy to accept unresolved claims. A legitimate `This graph cannot answer that question.` passes. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `meta_france_unsupported_phase`, `meta_missing_typed_phase`, `meta_missing_negation`, `meta_missing_count`, `no_antecedent_control`.
+
+### B8. Refused product spans become opaque entities, swallowing qualifiers and negation
+
+All with Q3; all return True; `[]`:
+
+- `osimertinib is liposomal.`
+- `osimertinib is a biosimilar.`
+- `Osimertinib is not liposomal.`
+- `liposomal Tagrisso.`
+
+The retrieved statement establishes only the EGFR action, not these product claims. `src/query.py:128-141` deliberately emits `Refused` spans and includes neighboring words. `src/claims.py:278-288` accepts them as entity placeholders; `:231-240` rejects `Missing` but does not reject `Refused`. The consumed span, including `not`, is no longer checked as wording or negation. In the Tagrisso example, the new alias exception (`query.py:495-499`, `:543-544`) admits the isolated brand token even though its enclosing product was refused. Sources: `standards_probe.json`, cases `refused_liposomal`, `refused_biosimilar`, `refused_negation`, `refused_alias`.
+
+### B9. Citation stripping still accepts arbitrary content after `ORG:`
+
+Q3 / `Osimertinib [ORG:is safe] acts on EGFR.` → True; `[]`.
+
+Q3 / `Osimertinib [ORG:inhibits ALK and] acts on EGFR.` → True; `[]`.
+
+The regex `ORG:[^\]]+` (`src/claims.py:136-137`) treats arbitrary bracket text as a citation, and `_units` deletes it at `:167`. It neither validates a source identifier nor checks membership in retrieved records. The ordinary R1 bracket example is now caught, but adding the citation prefix restores a text-deletion bypass. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `citation_embedded_claim`, `citation_embedded_wrong_relation`.
+
+## SHOULD FIX
+
+### S1. False positives are much lower on the original corpus, but ordinary faithful forms still fail
+
+The exact R1 corpus was rerun without rewriting its answers:
+
+| Demo question | R1 faithful answers flagged | R2 faithful answers flagged |
+|---|---:|---:|
+| Q1 | 2/6 | 0/6 |
+| Q2 | 2/6 | 0/6 |
+| Q3 | 1/6 | 0/6 |
+| Q4 | 3/6 | 0/6 |
+| Q5 | 2/6 | 1/6 |
+| Q6, refusals separately | 6/6 | 2/6 |
+
+Evidence-answer false positives improved from **10/30 (33.3%) to 1/30 (3.3%)**. This small hand-authored diagnostic corpus does not estimate a production LLM rate or support saying that most faithful answers fail. The remaining evidence-answer rejection is Q5 / `AstraZeneca supports trials studying osimertinib in NSCLC.` → False, `wording: supports`; the repair notes explicitly identify this as intentional strictness.
+
+Other confirmed faithful/refusal failures:
+
+| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q3 | `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and it inhibits EGFR.` | False; `no_antecedent: it` |
+| Q3 | `Osimertinib (Tagrisso) acts on EGFR.` | False; `wording: tagrisso` |
+| Q3 | `AZD 9291 acts on EGFR.` | False; `number: 9291` |
+| Q6 | `The graph covers NSCLC drugs and trials, so it cannot answer this question.` | False; `no_antecedent: it` |
+| Q6 | `I cannot determine the capital of France from the retrieved evidence.` | False; `entity: France`, `no_subject: i cannot determine capital france` |
+
+The first is the same within-unit antecedent timing error as B3. Repeated aliases are lost because the linker deduplicates by drug ID (`src/query.py:163-168`) before the tokenizer replaces all surface forms (`claims.py:281-288`). The spaced development code links correctly, but the global numeric pass does not exempt digits inside entity spans (`query.py:506-511`). Controls `Tagrisso acts on EGFR.` and `AZD9291 acts on EGFR.` pass. Evidence is in `replay_faithful_results.json`, `standards_probe.json`, and `spec_probe_results.json`.
+
+### S2. Documentation and the repair table still promise more than the code establishes
+
+- `README.md:508-520` and `README.ko.md:354-360` make categorical direction/action/ownership claims and say negations are always reported. B1–B9 contradict these claims. The acknowledged residual case—rearranging one evidence statement's words—does not explain discarded headings, source-like text, new words in refused spans, or stale antecedents. Even intended verbatim and META exceptions contradict “always”.
+- `src/claims.py:10-24` promises checked relations and reported unresolved pronouns; B1, B2, B3 and B7 refute those statements. `:15-17` says attribute evidence names every clause entity, whereas numeric `scoped()` (`:473-477`) uses only the first clause entity and heuristic owner.
+- `src/query.py:447` says a generated answer is verified to say nothing absent from evidence; `:461-466` retains categorical sentence-level guarantees. It was not updated to explain the clause parser's failure modes.
+- `web/template.html:263-265` and `web/try.html:69-71` retain categorical guarantees about unsupported relations/clinical claims. The softer success text at `web/try.html:142` is an improvement, but still says checked relations and values match when B1/B4/B5 demonstrate the contrary.
+- `CODEX_CLAIMS_R1_VERDICT.md:9` says the five R1 direction examples were repaired. The exact reversed noun still passes and is missing from `tests/test_claims.py:81-122`. Preserve the actual R1 cases, rather than treating the 33-case regression list as exhaustive.
+
+The independent standards review found no separate coding-standard document to enforce and no hard style violation. Its behavioral/documentation findings are recorded separately in [standards_findings.md](standards_findings.md); the independent spec analysis is in [spec_findings.md](spec_findings.md). Those axes do not substitute for each other.

+### Regressions, templates, linker and scope
+
+- **Tests:** `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` → **128 passed**. The brief's 79 is the older suite size. The initial run had a cp949 subprocess-reader warning; the UTF-8 rerun passed all 128 in 6.24s without warnings (`replay_pytest_utf8.txt`). TEMP/TMP stayed under `r2/replay_temp`, with bytecode and pytest cache disabled.
+- **Templates:** all **5/5 evidence template bodies** pass generated-prose checking after the same `Scope:` removal as the normal answer path. Full Scope-inclusive templates still fail. The France refusal template fails generated checking (`no_antecedent: it`, `no_subject: no entity question matched`); ordinary `ask()` uses its separate refusal path. All exact template texts/outputs are in `replay_faithful_results.json`.
+- **Original linker experiment:** **7,610 dictionary keys / 1,066 drugs / 2,634 unique multiword aliases**, zero lost intended links from the abbreviation gate. `is a` stays unlinked; `AZD 9291`, `osimertinib mesylate`, `trastuzumab deruxtecan`, and `pemetrexed disodium` retain intended links. This replays the original gate-on/gate-off experiment, not an old-commit execution.
+- **New punctuation handling:** there is no `link_entities` implementation change between 0c441e0 and a12762b; the new sanitization is in `claims.py:277`. `Osimertinib acts on EGFR!` passes. The question `Which drugs act on ALK!` (likewise `:` or `;`) still links no entity and yields `intent=unsupported`, while `?` works (`query.py:120`). This is an unaddressed question-path limitation, not a new linker regression.
+- **Dictionary-to-parser coverage:** among 2,634 stored multiword aliases, 2,566 link an intended drug ID; five of these lose that ID during claim tokenization (two parenthesized chemical aliases and three parent/salt overlap cases). Exact strings and entity traces are in `standards_probe.json`. These counts describe parser discrepancies, not five newly introduced linker regressions.
+- **Successful controls:** explicit reversed active relations, all original wrong named pairs, old clinical/negation attacks, Korean/mixed text, correct basic `target of`, correct `EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib`, and simple elided-subject `and inhibits` cases were exercised. Their passes/failures are retained alongside attacks. Fixes demonstrably improved behavior; the blockers remain independently reproducible.
+- **Dedup limit:** distinct-sentence contexts are fixed, but Q3 / `Osimertinib is safe and EGFR is safe.` still yields two raw `safe` findings and only one final finding because both clauses retain the same unit text (`query.py:560-562`). The result remains False; dedup cannot turn a nonempty findings list into a pass. See `root_design_probe.json`, ID `dedup_same_unit`.
+

New-design probes are `root_design_probe.py`, `spec_probe.py`, and `standards_probe.py`; their adjacent JSON files contain exact input, output and retrieved evidence. Final tracked/staged diffs were empty; `git status --short` listed only the pre-existing untracked `audit_evidence_claims/r2/` tree.

## BLOCKERS

Nine blocker groups remain. Examples returning **`grounded=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`**:

| Question | Unsupported answer |
|---|---|
| `What does osimertinib target?` | `EGFR is an osimertinib inhibitor.` |
